Document 017: 1天20倍！上周五买入英伟达期权的买家火了，周一放话“开盘即卖，感谢老黄”

Source PDF: all report pdf/1天20倍！上周五买入英伟达期权的买家火了，周一放话“开盘即卖，感谢老黄”.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-11-25
Pages: 2
Status: ok

### Page 1

⼀名 Reddit 散⼾以 1.9 万美元买⼊英伟达看涨期权，单⽇暴涨近 20 倍，净赚 37.69 万美元，
称此次交易让⾃⼰ “ ⼀笔赚到了年薪的 10 倍 ” 。机构同样豪赚 —— ⼀笔 1 ⽉到期的看涨期权仅
卖出半仓即锁定 2380 万美元收益。英伟达股价急涨后，机构已开始建⽴对冲头⼨，多空博
弈在新价位重启。
摘要：
⼀场押注英伟达反弹的期权交易，在周⼀早间因 CEO ⻩仁勋（ Jensen Huang ）重新执掌帅印⽽
迎来爆发式兑现。
据彭博 11 ⽉ 25 ⽇报道， Reddit ⽤⼾ Outrageous-Voice1459 以 1.9 万美元买⼊的英伟达看涨期权，
单⽇暴涨逾 1984% ，净赚 37.69 万美元，其在社交媒体上⾼呼 “ 开盘即卖，感谢⽼⻩ ” 的帖⼦迅速
引发⼴泛关注。
英伟达股价周⼀单⽇飙升 31% ⾄ 165.05 美元，此前该股本⽉累计跌幅已达 39% 。⻩仁勋重返
CEO 职位的消息成为直接催化剂，令此前布局反弹的多头期权持仓集中兑现。
这场期权盛宴不⽌于散⼾。据彭博报道，⼀名机构交易员此前买⼊ 1 ⽉到期、⾏权价 160 美元的

看涨期权，周⼀卖出其中⼀半仓位，单笔锁定收益达 2380 万美元。与此同时，股价⼤涨后，部
分⼤型机构也开始建⽴对冲头⼨，以防股价再度回落。
散⼾⼀战成名， “ 感谢⽼⻩ ” 刷屏 WallStreetBets
Reddit 论坛 r/WallStreetBets 上， Outrageous-Voice1459 晒出券商账⼾截图，称此次交易让⾃⼰
“ ⼀笔赚到了年薪的 10 倍 ” ，并留下那句⼴为流传的 “SOLD AT OPEN. THANK YOU JENSEN” 。
据彭博核实，该⽤⼾的交易⼊场细节与同⼀时间、同⼀价格在电⼦期权交易所发⽣的⼀笔真实
⼤宗交易记录相吻合，交易的真实性得到确认。
与此同时，社交媒体上另⼀笔 “ 神操作 ” 同样引发热议 —— 据称有⼈买⼊周⼀到期、⾏权价 160 美
元的看涨期权，并在⼤涨中获利颇丰。然⽽，据彭博对当⽇期权成交记录的核查，该笔交易并
⽆任何可查证的痕迹。
期权研究机构 VolSignals 联合创始⼈ Daniel Roos 表⽰：
机构早已布局，多策略押注反弹

散⼾的⾼光时刻背后，机构投资者的布局更为系统。据彭博报道，在英伟达股价持续下跌的过
程中，部分期权交易员已悄然将看涨头⼨移⾄更低的⾏权价，以更低成本换取更快兑现反弹收
益的机会。
另有机构买⼊数以万计的看涨期权及看涨价差组合，押注英伟达股价在明年初前回升；还有交
易员选择卖出看跌期权，愿意在股价进⼀步下跌时以约定价格接盘。
“ ⽬前没有证据证明这笔交易真实发⽣过。即便确有其事，在指数或热⻔标的出现如此剧烈波动
时，这类订单流也完全属于正常现象。 ”
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⻅闻历 搜索

### Page 2

⻛险提⽰及免责条款
市场有⻛险，投资需谨慎。本⽂不构成个⼈投资建议，也未考虑到个别⽤⼾特殊的投资⽬标、财务状况或需要。⽤⼾应考虑本⽂
中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。

图⽚
其中最具代表性的是那笔 1 ⽉到期、⾏权价 160 美元的看涨期权—— 持有⼈在周⼀股价飙升⾄
165.05 美元后，卖出⼀半仓位，单笔锁定 2380 万美元收益。
随着英伟达股价周⼀单⽇急涨 31% ，市场情绪迅速从押注反弹切换⾄防范回调。据彭博报道，
股价飙升后，部分⼤型交易员已开始建⽴新的对冲头⼨，以应对股价可能再度下滑的⻛险。
这⼀动向表明，尽管⻩仁勋回归提振了市场信⼼，但经历本⽉近四成跌幅后，机构对英伟达后
市⾛势仍保持审慎，多空博弈在新的价格区间内重新展开。

⽶国雇⽤は安定も、AI曝露度の⾼い職業で若年雇⽤が弱含
み。雇⽤不安が⺠主党躍進を後押しした可能性。AI関連株が
調整するなか、韓国中銀は投資減速のアジアへの波及を警戒
所定内給与は物価上昇率を上回る伸びが持続。冬季ボーナス
も押し上げ。電気・ガス料⾦補助再開は消費の追い⾵に。⼀⽅、
消費回復が進めば基調的な物価上昇圧⼒を強める可能性
注目点② 賃上げの波及と物価対策が日本の消費を下支え注目点① 米AI投資拡大の裏で広がる「不安」と「リスク」

に拡⼤圏を維持。雇⽤・企業活動の底堅さの⼀⽅

（出所）米労働省より、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成
失業率
米国：雇用統計は良好な結果。一方で、賃金上昇もインフレ見合いで見劣り
 10月の非農業部門雇用者数は前月差＋16.5万人と市場予想（同＋6.5万人）を大きく上回る結果
―過去2カ月も合わせて8.4万人の上方修正。業種別では専門ビジネスサービスやヘルスケアが全体をけん引
― 時間当たり賃金も前月比＋0.4％（前年比＋3.1％）と着実に増加。ただしインフレ率に割り負けている状況が継続
 失業率は4.1％と前月（4.2％）から小幅低下も、失業期間は長期化
― 失業期間の中央値は11.9週（9月：同11.5週）と長期化。27週以上の長期失業者も増加基調維持。企業の採用率は低下

傾向が続いており、労働者が失業状態から脱しにくい傾向が継続
3

（年）
米国：AI普及の影響は若年層に偏在。雇用機会への不安が拡大
 AI曝露度別の雇用指数をみると、22～34歳では曝露度の高い職業の雇用が相対的に弱含み
― AIの影響がキャリア形成段階の若年層に偏っている可能性
 若年層では、AIによる雇用代替に加え、物価高など生活面の不安も根強い状況
― AIを否定的に捉える割合も若い世代ほど高く、AI普及への警戒感がうかがえる状況
4
（注） 上図の若年層とは18～29歳。経済状況は回答者の主観的な評価

（出所） Harvard Youth Poll、ABC News、Washington Post、Ipsosより、みずほ総合研
究所調査部作成
AI曝露度別雇用指数
年齢別AIの認識：「AIは社会に悪い」と回答した割合
（注） 1．AI曝露度とは各職業の業務が生成AIの影響を受ける度合いを示す。職業を曝露度
に応じて5分位に分類し、低・中・高はそれぞれ第1・第3・第5分位を指す
2．年齢層別の雇用指数は、米国雇用統計の家計調査における年齢別雇用者数をウェ
イトに加重平均して算出。年齢区分が一致しない部分は、各階級内の雇用者数が均
等に分布すると仮定して按分
（出所）スタンフォード大学デジタル経済研究所、ADP Researchより、みずほ総合研究所調査
部作成

（投票先の割合、％）
（年/月）
米国：若年層の経済的不安が中間選挙の投票行動にも影響か
 2026年中間選挙では「物価高」や「雇用」への不安が若年層の主要な関心事項に
― こうした経済的不安が共和党政権への不満につながった可能性
― 年齢別の投票動向では、若年層を中心に民主党支持が優勢。生活費負担や将来の雇用機会への不安が投票行動に
影響した模様
― なお、議会でも世代交代が進展。若い世代の政策選好が今後の政策形成に与える影響にも注目

（参考）民主党が掲げる主な政策
 勝利した民主党は、若年層の不満とも重なる生活費負担の軽減（アフォーダビリティ）を共通の政策課題に位置付け
 具体的な政策手段を巡っては、中道派と左派・進歩派の間で違い
― 中道派は競争促進や規制緩和、財政規律を重視する一方、左派・進歩派は大企業への規制・課税強化や政府支出の
拡大を主張。今後の党内調整が焦点に

アジア：AI投資減速の影響が域内の貿易ネットワークを通じて波及するリスク
 韓国中銀はAI関連投資の拡大に伴う過剰投資やレバレッジの高まり、循環金融などの金融リスクを警戒
― AI産業の期待収益の過大評価や最終的なリスク所在把握が困難になり、リスクが過小評価される可能性があると指摘
― また、米国企業のAI投資が減速する悲観シナリオでは、韓国の半導体輸出が大幅に減少し、GDP成長率を0.4％Pt押し
下げると試算
― 韓国のAI関連財貿易は中国・台湾との取引が4割超。アジア域内で形成された貿易ネットワークを通じて、AI投資減速の
影響が広範に波及する可能性

日本：物価対策の再開が家計負担を軽減。消費の回復を後押し
9
 政府は冬場の電気・ガス料金補助の再開を決定。標準的な家庭で計7,000円程度の負担軽減を見込む
― 一方、ガソリン価格の補助は段階的に縮小（基準額を毎月5円ずつ引き上げ）。電気・ガス料金補助金の効果を一部減殺
 補助金を巡る政策変更は、コアCPIの前年比を最大0.4％程度押し下げ
― 賃金上昇に加え、政策による物価負担軽減が家計の実質購買力を下支え
― 物価対策がCPIを押し下げる一方、消費回復を通じて基調的な物価上昇圧力が強まる可能性には留意が必要

（注） ガソリンと同額の補助を実施している軽油・重油・灯油、ガソリン補助額の4割相当
の支援を実施している航空機燃料については未定

欧州：10月消費者物価はエネルギー主導で加速。企業は販売価格の引き上げを計画
10
 10月消費者物価（HICP）は前年比＋4.0％と、エネルギー主導で前月（同＋3.8％）から加速

中国：政策金利をリバースレポ翌日物に変更。銀行貸出金利の新たな決定メカニズムを模索
 中国人民銀行は政策金利をリバースレポ翌日物に変更（11/2）。短期市場金利の誘導精度を高める狙い
― リバースレポ7日物から切り替え、翌日物のオペを毎営業日実施。最優遇貸出金利（LPR）も翌日物金利を参照へ
― インターバンク市場における債券担保付レポ翌日物金利（DR001）が誘導目標と明言。短期金利の安定化を図る考え
 銀行貸出金利の新たな決定メカニズムを模索。DR001を参照して貸出金利を決定する方法を試験的に導入

― 背景には、銀行貸出の指標金利であるLPRが「最優遇」から乖離し、LPRより低利での貸出比率が上昇している実態
― 今後、財務力のある企業は銀行と協議の上でDR001かLPRを選択し、小規模企業や個人には引き続きLPRを適用へ

中国：景況感は再び悪化し、2カ月ぶりの節目割れ。製造業は需給ともに振るわず
 10月の景況感は再び悪化。総合PMIは49.7と前月（50.7）から大幅に低下し、2カ月ぶりの節目割れ
― うち非製造業PMIが49.7と前月（50.2）から低下も、宿泊やスポーツ・娯楽が持ち直し、情報サービスは堅調だった

 製造業PMIは49.3と前月（50.1）から低下。供給側の生産、需要側の受注がともに振るわず

― 生産が49.8（前月：51.7）、新規受注が49.4（同：50.5）、新規輸出受注も48.7（同：50.0）といずれも節目の50を下回る
― 中東情勢に伴う価格上昇に一服感。原材料価格は56.8（同：60.8）、出荷価格は51.7（同：54.0）まで戻す

 10月のPPIは前年比＋5.7％（前月：同＋5.0％）。中東情勢の影響による資源・エネルギー関連業種の価格上昇続く

― 食品・エネルギーを除くコアは同＋0.8％（前月：＋1.0％）と弱いが、通信機器はメモリー高騰で同＋16.1％の伸び

アジア：ASEAN5の製造業景況感は概ね堅調。原油高など仕入コスト上昇の影響は残存
 ASEAN5の10月の製造業景況感は、フィリピンを除き改善圏。受注・生産は好調も、コスト高が継続
― タイ、ベトナムは高水準で推移し、インドネシアは回復が鮮明。一方、フィリピンが基準となる50を下回る水準
― S&Pはリリース資料において、新規受注の増加が生産を支えたものの、仕入コスト上昇と原料調達・物流の制約を指摘
 ASEAN5では、生産者物価（PPI）が高止まり。原油高の影響が残存
― 原油高が続く中、石炭・石油に加えて化学やプラ・ゴム等の製品価格にも上昇圧力が継続
― 仕入コストの高止まりが、企業収益や生産活動の重荷となるリスクに留意が必要
14
（出所）LSEGより、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成

フィリピンを除き概ね堅調

### Page 16

 ASEAN5の一部の国で財政収支が悪化
― ベトナム、マレーシア、インドネシアで改善した一方で、フィリピンとタイで財政赤字が拡大し、明暗が分かれる結果に
― フィリピンは税収の伸び悩み、タイは景気下支えに向けた公共投資拡大や予算執行の加速などにより歳出が大きく増加
 各国とも財政面の制約を抱え、追加的な景気下支え余地は限定的
― タイは公的債務残高が法定上限（GDP比70％）に接近。マレーシアも政府債務残高が高水準
― フィリピンは財政赤字の縮小が課題。インドネシアは財政赤字上限（GDP比3％）が制約要因
アジア：ASEAN5の財政収支は明暗が分かれるも、追加的な財政出動余地は限定的

はIMFの名目GDP予測値を使用

市場焦點
美國
• 10月零售銷售月增0.2%，整體增幅略緩，但與GDP高度相關的控制組零售銷售月增0.6%，顯
示高利率環境下消費需求仍具韌性，內需仍是支撐美國經濟的重要力量，然而可支配所得成
長趨緩、儲蓄率維持低檔，且家庭支出連續多月高於薪資成長，反映部分消費來自信用擴張
而非所得改善。整體而言，美國消費短期仍支撐經濟，但家庭購買力壓力正逐漸積累，未來
內需續航力仍有待觀察。
中國
• H200晶片的出口管制反覆政策變化，促使中國企業加速開發本土替代產品，加上中國經濟正
從房地產開發，轉向高科技帶動經濟成長，彭博預估2026年中國高科技產業的規模將達到27
兆元人民幣，占GDP的18.3%。總結來說，美中科技戰將使全球AI 供應鏈逐漸形成兩套體系，
以美國為核心，另一套以中國自主晶片為核心，長期而言這將改變半導體、AI 伺服器及資料中

心產業的競爭格局。
AI
資本支出
• 半導體研究機構Semi Analysis下修未來數年全球AI加速器出貨、資本支出、HBM需求及電
力需求預測，反映AI產業正由GPU主導的高速擴張階段，逐步轉向多元運算架構與成本效率
競爭，預估輝達受到的衝擊相對較大；博通、AMD，以及Google TPU、Amazon Trainium
等平台，則因客製化晶片與成本效益優勢，展現相對韌性。這並不代表ASIC將全面取代GPU，
而是AI算力市場的供應來源將更加多元化。

### Page 5

兆豐銀行所做任何投資意見與市場分析結果，係依據資料製作當時情況進行判斷

消費韌性延續、購買力承壓，美國內需強勁背後隱憂浮現
• 整體零售增幅放緩，核心消費仍具韌性：美國10月零售銷售月增0.2%，低於9月的0.8%，但與市場預期相符；與GDP
消費支出較相關的控制組零售銷售月增0.6%，優於預估0.4%，且9月數據上修，顯示核心消費仍具支撐力，非店面零售、
建材及醫療保健用品銷售維持成長，反映美國內需尚未明顯轉弱，仍是支撐經濟成長的重要動能。
• 實質所得成長受限，家庭消費續航力面臨考驗：零售銷售成長仍須留意物價因素，10月名目銷售增幅未必代表實質消費
同步擴張；9月實質可支配所得月減0.3%，儲蓄率降至3.8%，顯示家庭購買力與儲蓄緩衝能力承壓，儘管低收入家庭近
期支出仍具韌性，但通膨侵蝕實質所得，加上高利率環境持續，後續消費動能仍面臨考驗。

• 後市觀察：美國消費短期仍具韌性，但物價與融資成本偏高，可能逐步壓抑非必要性支出；建議股票優先布局具品牌優
勢、定價能力及獲利穩定的民生必需品與醫療保健類股，債券則以短天期美債及高信評債券為主，降低升息環境下的價
格波動風險。

市
場
回
顧
美國通膨黏著依舊，Fed緊縮政策提高升息預期
• 通膨來源輪動，核心PCE動能再度升溫：彭博經濟研究預估10月核心PCE月增0.29%、年增3.2%，三個月年化增幅由
2.9%升至3.5%。儘管AI相關硬體、軟體及機票價格回落，有助抵銷部分通膨壓力，但處方藥、餐飲服務、地面運輸及部
分商品價格接續上漲，使核心通膨維持高檔，顯示通膨壓力持續輪動，尚未有效消退。
• 通膨黏著性升高，市場轉向升息定價：過去一年漲幅逾3%的PCE分項占比預估由49%升至51%，反映通膨改善仍不穩固；
儘管6個月年化口徑略降至45%，然短期通膨動能仍有回升跡象，加上10月PPI年增率升至6.5%，以及近期Fed官員釋出
進一步緊縮訊號，市場已提高12月升息及2027年初持續升息的預期，利率維持高檔的時間可能拉長。

• 市場看法：在通膨黏著性與升息預期同步升高下，美債殖利率及美元可能維持相對強勢，長天期債券與高評價成長股面
臨較大壓力；債券建議優先配置短天期美債、浮動利率債及高信評投資等級債，股票則以獲利穩健、現金流充足及具定
價能力的企業為核心，藉以降低因市場波動而衍生的風險。
10月核心PCE動能料反彈
資料來源：BEA, BLS, Bloomberg Economics，2026.11.17。
華許關注的PCE通膨指標10月可能未現改善

升息預期升溫、緊縮效應浮現，美債布局契機逐步醞釀
• 升息預期升溫，殖利率走高提升債券收益吸引力：Fed官員近
期密集釋出偏鷹訊號，10月FOMC會議紀錄亦顯示，多數官員
認為若通膨未能有效降溫，仍有必要進一步升息，部分官員甚
至認為不排除採取更大幅度的升息措施，市場因此提高對12月
升息的預期，10年期美債殖利率於11月中旬升至5.1%左右，
反映市場對利率維持高檔及進一步緊縮的預期；惟從債券投資
角度來看，殖利率走高也同步提升新投入資金的收益水準，若
後續通膨壓力逐步緩解，將有機會為債券價格回升創造條件。
• 緊縮政策壓抑需求，經濟降溫有望帶動債市轉機：美國經濟目
前仍受AI相關資本支出及企業投資支撐，但高利率環境將逐步
提高企業融資成本、壓抑消費與投資動能，若經濟成長因此放

緩，並帶動通膨壓力降溫，市場對未來政策利率的預期便可能
由升息轉向持平甚至下修，進而推動美債殖利率回落、債券價
格回升。尤其當經濟降溫疑慮升高時，美國公債的避險需求亦
可能增加，為短中天期債券帶來布局機會。
• 市場看法：後續應持續關注12月FOMC會議決策、通膨及就業
數據，以及升息對企業融資與民間需求的影響。建議採取穩健
防禦、分批布局策略，優先留意短中天期美國公債及投資等級
債，在殖利率相對高檔時掌握較具吸引力的收益水準；待通膨
降溫、實質殖利率回落及降息預期逐步形成，再視市場變化適
度調整存續期間，以兼顧債息收益與後續資本利得機會。

中美科技脫鉤不可能逆轉
美中科技戰升級，中國半導體自主化進程加速
• 美中科技戰升級，中國半導體自主化進程加速：輝達原本向中國銷售H200晶片，由黃仁勳與川普協商促成，根據該協
議，晶片必須經由美國運送，讓美國政府可以收取25%的關稅，近日美國公布收緊對中國AI晶片出口限制，輝達也停止
向中國銷售H200晶片，顯示美中科技戰進一步升級，與此同時，中國其實早已積極推動半導體自主化，降低對美國技
術的依賴，隨著美國封堵海外資料中心提供中國AI算力的管道，中國勢必加速採用國產替代方案，華為有望成為最大受
益者，華為昇騰系列AI處理器是輝達硬體的替代選擇。
• 展望後市：H200晶片的出口管制反覆政策變化，促使中國企業加速開發本土替代產品，加上中國經濟正從房地產開發，
轉向高科技帶動經濟成長，彭博預估2026年中國高科技產業的規模將達到27兆元人民幣，占GDP的18.3%。總結來說，

美中科技戰將使全球AI 供應鏈逐漸形成兩套體系，以美國為核心，另一套以中國自主晶片為核心，長期而言這將改變
半導體、AI 伺服器及資料中心產業的競爭格局。

地產銷售與開工增速仍處低位
商品房銷售面積 房屋新開工面積 房屋竣工面積
房地產銷售和開工偏弱，開發投資承壓
• 房地產銷售和開工偏弱，開發投資承壓：2026年前三季中國房地產市場仍處於調整階段，整體復甦力道不足，1至9月房
地產開發投資年減20.6%，新建商品房銷售面積年減13.2%，顯示建商投資與購屋需求依然疲弱，雖然部分核心城市房市
出現改善跡象，但尚未有效帶動全國市場回溫，預估第四季局部市場的好轉，對全國開發投資的提振作用有限，從交易
結構來看，新房與二手房市場持續分化，9月新房銷售面積降幅較前期擴大，而二手房成交面積則年增10.7%，顯示購屋
需求轉為價格較具優勢的二手市場，價格方面，一線城市二手住宅價格9月較上月上漲，新房與二手房價格維持正成長，
但漲幅逐漸收斂，但是二、三線城市房價仍呈波動整理，尚未形成明顯回升趨勢。

• 展望後市：中國房地產開發投資累計年增率降幅，可望較2026年進一步收斂，房市銷售回暖不代表投資立即回升，當成
交改善後，房企往往優先將資金用於提升現金流、推進在建工程及履行交屋責任，以降低財務壓力並穩定營運，唯有當
住宅去化速度持續，因此市場預估2027年房地產市場將延續築底，整體呈現「銷售先穩、投資後升」的溫和修復格局。

Semi Analysis下調AI算力資本開支
• Semi Analysis下修AI資本支出預測：半導體研究機構Semi Analysis於11月13日更新AI產業模型，下修未來數年全球AI加
速器出貨、資本支出、HBM需求及電力需求預測，其中2028至2030年調整幅度最為明顯。全球AI加速器資本支出預測分
別下調28%、41%及40%，2030年預測值由原先的1.531兆美元降至9,230億美元。
• AI收入增速放緩，融資環境成為主要壓力：此次下修主要反映AI商業化進展與資金供給不如預期。Semi Analysis指出，前
沿AI實驗室第三季新增年度經常性收入（ARR）增速放緩，使市場重新評估未來Token收入能否持續支撐龐大的算力成本；
另一方面，債權與股權投資者對AI基礎設施的融資意願降低，尤其缺乏大型雲端服務商長期採購承諾的專案，面臨較高的

建設與資金風險。此外，美國中期選舉後，資料中心監管及審批不確定性增加，預計部分原訂2027年下半年至2028年上
半年投產的資料中心將延後。不過，Semi Analysis認為，AI收入成長放緩與融資環境收緊，才是本次預測下修的主要原因。

硬體需求同步下修，供應鏈回歸正常化
• 硬體需求同步下修，供應鏈回歸正常化：除資本支出外，Semi Analysis亦下調AI硬體需求預測，2026至2030年全球AI加
速器邏輯晶片出貨面積累計下修17%；HBM方面，2028年出貨量預測由9.0EB降至6.4EB，下修30%，反映供需逐步改善
及價格漲勢趨緩。此外，2030年AI加速器功耗需求預測由122.8GW降至80.7GW，下修34%。整體而言，AI硬體需求仍
持續成長，但先前由晶片短缺、記憶體漲價及高額資本支出推動的擴張模式，正逐步回歸較合理的供需水準。
• AI產業進入效率競爭，資本支出仍具成長空間：整體而言，Semi Analysis此次下修並不代表AI需求消失，而是市場開始
重新檢視AI投資的商業回報、融資可持續性及資料中心建置效率。未來AI產業發展將不再單純追求算力規模擴張，而是更

重視運算成本、能源效率與投資報酬率。儘管AI基礎設施資本支出仍將維持成長，但成長速度可能低於先前預期，且資金
將更集中於具備明確客戶需求、穩定融資來源及較佳成本效益的建置計畫。

AI晶片供應商：輝達面臨最大下修壓力
公司 Semi Analysis 主要看法 影響與評估
輝達 未來出貨量預測遭大幅下修；大型AI實驗室轉向其他運算平台 加上Neocloud客戶融資風險提高，新一代晶片亦面臨降價壓力 本次下修幅度最大 未來營收與毛利率可能同時承壓
博通 2026至2030年ASIC業務累計營收預測下修11% 主要反映HBM成本下降帶動售價調整，而非大幅削減出貨量 相對具韌性 受惠Google TPU及大型AI客戶自研ASIC需求，性能成本比與能效具優勢
AMD 2028至2030年出貨及資本支出預測遭下修 受惠Meta、OpenAI及Anthropic等大型客戶布局，部分新產品部署時程提前 相較輝達具一定韌性 但中長期仍面臨AI需求及資本支出下修風險

雲端平台與AI模型業者：自研晶片及運算成本成為關鍵
公司 Semi Analysis 主要看法 影響與評估
亞馬遜 Trainium在2027及2028年的出貨預測未遭下修，主因資料中心專案儲備充足、 晶片製造成本較低，且願意透過策略性補貼支持重要客戶 少數維持近端成長預期的平台，自研ASIC有助降低對外部GPU的依賴
Google TPU具備性能成本比及能源效率優勢 是大型AI實驗室採購的重要替代方案 自研ASIC有助降低運算成本，提升AI基礎設施競爭力
OpenAI 除持續使用GPU，也將AMD GPU及自研ASIC納入運算平台布局 算力來源逐步多元化，有助降低對單一GPU供應商的依賴
Anthropic 採用TPU、Trainium、AMD GPU及輝達GPU等四大運算平台；從性能成本 比考量，輝達GPU相對處於較不具優勢的位置 有利於具備成本及能效優勢的ASIC，替代GPU平台
Meta 與AMD透過認股權證安排建立合作關係，並布局客製化AI晶片 有助AMD取得大型客戶訂單，大型科技公司積極推動算力來源多元化

Semi Analysis：成長放緩但未停止
成長放緩，而非停止
2030年資本支出仍達9,230億美元，高於2026年的3,920億美元。
競爭焦點轉移
從單純性能比拼，轉向性能成本比、能效與融資能力的綜合較量。
1
2
3 輝達壓力大於部分ASIC供應商
地位仍強，但成長面臨更大壓力；AMD、博通與亞馬遜Trainium相對穩健。
觀察面向 SemiAnalysis釋出的訊號
GPU市場 輝達仍具市場領先地位，但大型客戶開始重視成本效益，降低對單一GPU平台的依賴。
ASIC市場 Google TPU、Amazon Trainium及客製化ASIC逐漸取得競爭優勢。
AI資本支出 AI收入成長放緩及融資環境收緊，使資料中心投資計畫面臨重新評估。
Neocloud 缺乏大型雲端業者長期採購承諾的業者，融資與專案執行風險較高。

產業競爭 未來競爭核心將由單純追求算力規模，轉向性能成本比、能源效率及融資能力。

### Page 14

兆豐銀行所做任何投資意見與市場分析結果，係依據資料製作當時情況進行判斷
惟可能因市場變化而變動，投資標的之價格與收益將隨時變動，亦不必然為未來績效表現 14

經理人經濟軟著陸成主流共識
• 根據11月份美銀美林經理人調查報告顯示經濟軟著陸成主流共識，過去引領全球股市狂飆的人工智慧(AI)題材正進
入壓力測試期，資金開始從高度集中的科技股撤出，轉向具備防禦性與合理估值的板塊。
• AI資本支出受檢視，科技股減碼創20年之最：過去市場無條件追捧AI概念股的風氣已經改變，法人機構開始用放大
鏡檢視企業的變現能力與財務體質，AI投資從擴張轉向檢視，高達淨38%的經理人認為目前企業在AI領域投資過度，
創下調查歷史新高；更有52%經理人預期未來半年內將有超大規模雲端服務商宣布削減資本支出。同時，更希望企
業將現金流用於改善資產負債表(42%)或增加股東回饋(33%)，僅16%支持繼續擴大資本支出。
• 尾端風險和最擁擠交易：AI泡沫(34%)與AI超大規模雲端服務商信用事件(31%)包辦市場前兩大尾端風險；此外，

38%經理人擔憂大型雲端商發行的債券可能成為系統性信用風險來源。作多全球半導體雖仍是最擁擠交易，但認同
度已從7月高峰的82%大幅滑落至39%，撤出的資金明顯轉向防禦型的醫療類股(淨加碼升至34%，躍居最受青睞產
業)、債券(減碼幅度由淨37%收斂至淨23%)以及消費類股。展望2027年，經理人最看好的資產類別依序為國際股票
(36%)、美股(24%)與黃金(17%)。
• 小結及建議：整體而言，在經濟溫和軟著陸背景下，投資策略建議多元配置、股債兼備以抵抗波動。
資料來源：美銀美林證券，富蘭克林投顧整理，2026.11

資人申購基金係持有基金受益憑證，而非本資料提及之投資資產或標的。

実質ＧＤＰ成長率の推移

（資料）内閣府経済社会総合研究所「四半期別ＧＤＰ速報」
( )内は年率換算値
(注）2602までは当研究所による改定見込値、2603は予測値
予測

１. 11/16に内閣府から公表される 2026年 7-9月期の実質 GDPは、前期比▲0.1％（前期比年
率▲0.3％）と 4四半期ぶりのマイナス成長になったと推計される。 
２. 高水準の企業収益を背景に設備投資が前期比 1.4％と大幅に増加したが、天候不順の影響
などから民間消費が前期比▲0.3％と減少したこと、原油を中心に輸入が大幅に増加し、
外需が成長率を大きく押し下げたことなどから、小幅ながら 4四半期ぶりのマイナス成
長になったとみられる。 
３. 名目 GDPは前期比 0.5％（前期比年率 2.0％）と 10四半期連続で増加し、実質 GDPの伸
びを大きく上回るだろう。GDPデフレーターは前期比 0.6％、前年比 2.5％と予測する。 
４. 2026年 7-9月期のマイナス成長の一因となった民間消費の減少は、天候不順による一時
的な影響が大きい。ただし、こうした影響が解消されても、物価上昇の加速が消費の回
復を妨げる可能性が高い。 
５. 現時点では、2026年 10-12月期はプラス成長に復帰するものの、民間消費が伸び悩むこ
となどから、前期比年率 0％台半ばとマイナス成長の後としては低めの伸びにとどまる
と予想している。 
ニッセイ基礎研究所 2026-10-30

2026年 7-9月期の実質 GDP 
～前期比▲0.1％（年率▲0.3％）を予測～

経済研究部 経済調査部長 斎藤  太郎 
(03)3512-1836  tsaito@nli-research.co.jp

### Page 2

名目 GDPは前期比 0.5％（前期比年率 2.0％）と 10四半期連続で増加し、実質 GDPの伸びを大
きく上回るだろう。GDPデフレーターは前期比 0.6％（4-6月期：同 1.0％）、前年比 2.5％（4-6月
期：同 2.6％）と予測する。

なお、11/16に内閣府から 2026年 7-9月期の GDP速報が発表される際には、基礎統計の改定や季
節調整のかけ直しなどから、成長率が過去に遡って改定される。当研究所では、2026年 4-6月期の
実質 GDP成長率は、外需、設備投資が上方修正されることから、前期比年率 1.4％から同 2.3％へ
上方修正されると予想している。

2026年 7-9月期のマイナス成長の一因となった民間消費の減少は、天候不順による一時的な影響
が大きい。ただし、こうした影響が解消されても、物価上昇の加速が消費の回復を妨げる可能性が
高い。消費者物価上昇率は 2026年 9月に 9ヵ月ぶりの 2％台となったが、原材料価格の上昇を耐久
財や日用品などの販売価格に転嫁する動きが今後さらに広がることから、2026年度末にかけて 3％
台まで高まる公算が大きい。物価上昇率の高まりによる実質購買力の低下を背景に 2026年度後半
の民間消費は弱めの動きが続くことが予想される。 
現時点では、2026年 10-12月期はプラス成長に復帰するものの、民間消費が伸び悩むことなどか
ら、前期比年率 0％台半ばとマイナス成長の後としては低めの伸びにとどまると予想している。

1 10/30までに公表された経済指標をもとに予測している。今後公表される経済指標の結果によって予測値を修正する可
能性がある。

### Page 3

民間消費は前期比▲0.3％と予測する。 
実質賃金はプラスの伸びが続いているが、物価上昇率の高まりによって実質購買力が押し下げら
れていることに加え、台風や豪雨、長雨などの天候不順が個人消費を下押しした。 
2026年 7-9月期の消費関連指標を確認すると、延べ宿泊者数（うち日本人）は前期比 2.8％（4-6
月期：同▲0.9％）の増加となったが、自動車税の
「環境性能割」の廃止を受けて 4-6月期に前期比
10.7％の高い伸びとなった自動車販売台数が、その
反動で同▲7.5％と落ち込んだほか、外食産業売上
高が前期比▲0.3％（4-6月期：同 0.2％）、小売業
販売額指数が前期比▲2.1％（4-6月期：同 0.6％）
と減少した（いずれもニッセイ基礎研究所による
季節調整値、外食産業売上高、小売業販売額指数
は消費者物価指数で実質化）。

・住宅投資～弱めの動きが続く～

住宅投資は前期比▲0.2％と 2四半期連続の減
少を予測する。 
新設住宅着工戸数（季節調整済・年率換算値）
は、建築物省エネ法・建築基準法改正前の駆け
込み需要の反動で、2025年 1-3月期の 86.7万戸
から 4-6月期に 62.0万戸へ急減した後、7-9月
が 71.9万戸と持ち直し、その後は概ね 70万戸
台半ばで推移している。 
GDP統計の住宅投資は住宅価格上昇の影響も
あり弱めの動きとなっている。

・民間設備投資～高水準の企業収益を背景に増加～

民間設備投資は前期比 1.4％と 3四半期ぶりの増加を予測する。 
設備投資の一致指標である資本財総供給は 2026年 4-6月期の前期比 4.9％の後、7-9月期も同 4.9％
となった。また、機械投資の先行指標である機械受注（船舶・電力を除く民需）は 2026年 4-6月
期に前期比 0.2％と 3四半期連続で増加した後、7、8月の平均は 4-6月期を 0.4％上回っている。 
日銀短観 2026年 9月調査では、2026年度の設備投資計画（含むソフトウェア・研究開発投資額、
除く土地投資額）が 6月調査から 0.5％上方修正され、前年度比 9.3％（全規模・全産業）となった。 
設備投資は、高水準の企業収益を背景に、人手不足対応の省力化投資、デジタル化に向けた情報

2026年度

・公的固定資本形成～2四半期連続の減少～

公的固定資本形成は、前期比▲2.4％と予測する。 
公共工事の先行指標である公共工事請負金額
は 2026年 7-9月期に前年比▲2.6％と 6四半期ぶ
りに減少した。また、公共工事の進捗を反映する
公共工事出来高（建設総合統計）は、2026年 4-6
月期に前年比 5.2％と 9四半期連続で増加した後、
2026年 7、8月の平均は同 2.4％と伸びが鈍化し
ている。 
2025年度補正予算による押し上げ効果が弱ま
っていることに加え、2026 年度当初予算の公共
事業関係費の伸びが抑えられていることから、弱
めの動きとなっている。

・外需～4四半期ぶりのマイナス寄与～

外需寄与度は前期比▲0.4％（前期比年率▲1.8％）と 4四半期ぶりのマイナスを予測する。財貨・
サービスの輸出が前期比 2.0％、財貨・サービスの輸入が同 4.8％となるだろう。代替調達の進展に
伴う原油輸入量の急増を主因として輸入が高い伸
びとなり、輸出の伸びを上回ったことから、外需は
成長率の押し下げ要因となった。 
2026年 7-9月期の地域別輸出数量指数を季節調整
値（当研究所による試算値）でみると、米国向けが
前期比 0.9％（4-6月期：同 2.8％）、EU向けが前期
比▲2.4％（4-6月期：同 0.3％）、アジア向けが前期
比▲0.3％（4-6 月期：同▲0.8％）、うち中国向けが

Status: ok

### Page 1

Taiwan Information Technology

What’s new: We review Lotes’s 3Q26 results, released on 13 November.

What’s the impact: Business outlook. Ongoing gross margin dilution due 
to rising raw material costs and less benefits from the AI server trend has 
been, in our view, the primary drag on Lotes’s share price over this year. 
We believe this trend may reverse now as investors are worried about the 
potential AI capex cuts impacting the overall Taiwan supply chain’s revenue 
outlook in 2028, whereas Lotes only has 3-5% of exposure currently. Also, 
management expects gross margin pressure to start easing with the new 
product mass production (eg, SOCAMM) starting from 4Q26. Second, 
Lotes’s regular server-related products may see less chance for inventory 
correction as its products are not in shortage now. Lastly, its desktop 
business has suffered more than the overall industry’s headwinds (ie, unit 
decline 10-15% YoY) as its desktop products had to downgrade from DDR5 
to DDR4/3 due to component shortages, which further negatively impacted 
profitability. We believe these three headwinds will gradually transform into 
tailwinds once the AI capex starts to slow down. However, if the AI server 
capex trend remains as is, we could also see some upside for the 
company. Lotes is still eager to develop a GPU socket for the Rubin Ultra 
generation, which we believe could become a new growth driver. Besides, 
quick disconnect (QD) and NPO sockets might also become growth drivers. 
While QD only accounts for low single digit of total revenue from 3Q26, we 
expect it to have a chance to increase to c.10% of total revenue in 2027E.

3Q26 profitability missed expectations. Lotes’s 3Q26 earnings came in 
at TWD1.92bn (EPS: TWD17.1), which was 11%/14% lower than our/street 
estimates. Despite revenue largely in-line with expectations, gross margin 
decreased by 0.6pp QoQ to 44.8%, mainly from rising copper and gold 
prices; unfavourable product mix (rising high-speed cable and connect and 
QD) may be another reason. However, operating margin rose 0.9pp QoQ to 
22.9% mainly on the lack of one-off employee stock bonus expense (as 
done in 2Q26) but opex was still higher than the company’s guidance 
(<TWD2.0bn) due to salary increase for its Chinese branches.

What we recommend: We lower 2026-27E EPS by 2-7% to factor in 3Q26 
results and lower profitability recovery assumption but maintain 2028E 
EPS. We reaffirm our Buy (1) call but lower our 12M TP to TWD2,480 (from 
TWD2,850), based on a lower PER of 22x (from 28x; high end of its past-5-
year range of 10-28x), on our 1-year forward EPS, given the weaker market 
sentiment. Key risk: lower-than-expected adoption of server CPU platforms.

How we differ: Our 2027-28E revenues are 5-10% higher than the 
Bloomberg consensus, likely due to our more bullish view on QDs.

13 November 2026

3Q26 review: bottomed-out gross margin

➢ 3Q GM slightly declined on rising material cost but likely bottomed out 
➢ Rising QD, SOCAMM, NPO sockets to be key growth drivers in 2027 
➢ Reiterating our Buy (1) rating; lowering 12-month TP to TWD2,480

Company profile 
Founded in 1986 and listed on the Taiwan Stock Exchange in 2007, Lotes is one of the world’s leading 
connector vendors specialising in CPU socket/ILM and connectors, such as DDR, PCIe, SATA, and 
Type-C, with most of its production capacities at its Guangzhou, Zhongshan and Suzhou plants. Its 
superior manufacturing capability and strong client relationships are the key differentiators that make 
Lotes stand out from its competitors. Since the PC market became saturated from 2015, Lotes has 
focused on penetrating non-PC connector businesses, such as connectors for servers, automotive and 
industrial, and new types of cables (eg, Thunderbolt cable).

### Page 5

5

Lotes (3533 TT): 13 November 2026 
ESG analysis

Update Date: 13 Nov 2026 
Source: Daiwa, Company

ESG risks
Risks Management Analyst comments
G
Executive/board
quality 2
Lotes’ board consists of 7 directors, including 3 independent directors. The total number of
directors is slightly lower than other listed companies in our coverage. Lotes held 8 board
meetings in 2025 and the actual attendance rate was 95%.
Although the Chairman and CEO are not the same person, they are, in fact, brothers and the co-
founders of Lotes. It gives us a sense that Lotes is still a family enterprise, which is likely a
negative for the board and executive management. That said, the background of independent
directors is diversified with expertise in technology and management. We see it as a positive for
the board's quality.
Capital management 2
Lotes declared a DPS of TWD41.5 in 2025, representing a payout ratio of 50%. Being a leading
connector vendor for applications such as desktops, notebooks and servers, Lotes grew rapidly
along with its key clients (Intel and AMD) on strong technical capabilities and higher growth rate
from the server market. The solid revenue CAGR in the past decade required higher working
capital and continuous capex to expand its capacity, which could also be the culprit for its slightly
lower payout ratio compared with other electronic component makers. Having said that, we think
Lotes’ higher revenue growth should partially offset the drawback of a lower payout ratio.
Related party &
transaction 1
In 2025, there were no disclosed related-party sales, while purchases from related parties
amounted to TWD4.4m, representing 0.03% of total cost of sales. We see limited risk from
related-party transactions.
S Supply chain management 2
Lotes established its RBA management system in 2010 and requires its suppliers to meet the
standards of no child labour, no forced labour, treating employees with respect and dignity, and
caring for labourers' safety and health. Lotes hosts a yearly supplier conference to promote the
RBA guidelines to its suppliers. In 2025, 187 suppliers participated in the annual supplier
conference. Lotes also requires its suppliers to sign the RBA-related commitment letters. For
better supply chain management, Lotes conducts yearly audits and regular business reviews
and holds the yearly supplier conference as measures. In 2025, Lotes conducted written
evaluations of 110 suppliers and on-site audits of 61 suppliers. In addition, all 76 newly added
suppliers passed its environmental and social responsibility evaluations. Lotes has required new
suppliers to sign the "Supplier Social and Environmental Responsibility Commitment Letter"
since 2010 to extend social responsibility concepts and requirements to the supply chain.
For "green" purchase management, Lotes has adopted the IECQ QC 080000 hazardous
substance process management system. Lotes asks its suppliers to follow the RoHS, HSF, and
REACH guidelines to avoid using hazardous materials. Lotes also conducts conflict minerals due
diligence and requires suppliers to sign non-conflict mineral declarations and complete surveys
tracing mineral sources to smelters. Overall, Lotes believes it has constructed a comprehensive
system covering system certification (e.g., ISO 9001), product design, supply chain
management, and production techniques to ensure that the entire manufacturing process, from
raw materials to end products, follows green principles. All products from Lotes are now 100%
compliant with RoHS standards.
S Data security 2
Lotes has established a team to implement data security regulations, planning and maintenance.
Moreover, it monitors vulnerabilities in its information systems each year through regular
vulnerability scanning, access control reviews and information security audits. The company
reported no losses resulting from information security incidents in 2025.
Note: Management score represents a company's ability to manage/benefit from certain ESG topics. The scores range from 1 to 3, with 1 being the strongest.

Greater China Technology Semiconductors
Asia Pacific
Industry View Attractive
Limited direct impact on Aspeed and Montage in the near term,

but we see potential long-term demand risks.
The US BIS has tightened export controls to restrict Chinese companies' remote
access to advanced computing capacity through third-country data centers, while
adding licensing requirements for advanced chips shipped to key Southeast Asian
markets, including Singapore and Malaysia (link).
Our view: Gary Yu (China Internet Analyst; link) sees this as another step toward
closing loopholes in Al compute access, potentially slowing GPU deployment by

China-linked cloud operators and raising compliance costs for overseas Al
infrastructure investment. New supplies of advanced chipsets within China to
become increasingly limited. For cloud semis, we see limited direct impact on
Aspeed and Montage in the near term but there are potential long-term demand
risks. Aspeed has a high share in GPU and China server platforms. Montage
interface and retimer chips may also be impacted over the long term.
Please also refer to our recent notes: Cloud Semis: Peak Growth, Less Upside;

Investor pushback on our cloud semis/old memory downgrades.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.

營收走勢圖

研究員/丁宥霖 yulin.ting@uni-psg.com

貿聯 3Q26 財 報 優 於 預 期 ， 營 業 利 益 率 創 歷 史 新 高 ， 加 上 
Interplex 併表效益優於原先預估，為後續營運增添新亮點。展
望未來，研究部持續看好貿聯中長期成長潛力，主要考量：(1) 隨 
AI 機櫃功耗持續提升，電力傳輸規格朝高電流、高壓化發展，帶
動 Power Whip、Busbar 等產品升級，單櫃電源產值可望倍增；
(2) 高速互連朝 1.6T 以上演進，AEC 與高階光纖業務接續放
量，推升資料傳輸產值；(3) Interplex 併購補足精密機構能力，
結合既有電力及高速互連布局，貿聯可望由零組件供應商逐步延
伸至機櫃次系統整合，擴大單一客戶供應範圍及長期成長商機。
研究部將 Interplex 納入預估，上修 2026、2027 年稀釋後 EPS 
至 73.49 元（+67.4% YoY，原估 68.77 元）及 145.24 元
（+103.2% YoY，原估 117.82 元）。惟考量近期 AI 資料中心建
置不確定性升高，可能使新平台導入與機櫃出貨時程遞延，壓抑
評價表現，研究部將本益比由 25 倍下修至 20 倍，以 2027 年 
EPS 為評價基礎，目標價 2,950 元，投資評等由「買進」調升至
「強力買進」。

重點摘要： 
1. 3Q26 營業利益率創歷史新高，財報優於預期 
貿聯 3Q26 營收 278.75 億元（+19.7% QoQ、+51.4% YoY），符
合預期。其中 HPC 營收成長強勁（+30% QoQ、+61% YoY），營收
占比由 2Q26 的 42% 提升至 46%。隨 AEC 拉貨恢復正常，上半
年產線未達經濟規模的不利影響逐步消除，帶動整體毛利率回升
至 32.53%（+1.80ppt QoQ、−0.72ppt YoY）；加上營收規模擴
大，營業費用率降至 11.75%（−1.76ppt QoQ、−1.97ppt YoY），
推升營業利益率至 20.78%（+3.56ppt QoQ、+1.24ppt YoY），創
歷史新高。營業利益達 57.92 億元（+44.5% QoQ、+61.0% YoY），
優於原估的 52.50 億元。業外因匯兌損失及利息費用合計損失 
2.30 億元，稅後淨利達 42.90 億元（+43.9% QoQ、+63.2% YoY），
優於原估的 39.78 億元，稀釋後 EPS 為 20.34 元，優於原估
的 19.45 元。

### Page 2

請參見末頁重要批露及免責聲明  2

2. Interplex 併表貢獻超預期，上修 4Q26 營收與獲利 
貿聯 10 月營收 118.46 億元（+26.9% MoM、+80.0% YoY），創
歷史新高，主要受惠 Interplex 於 9 月底完成交割，10 月首
度完整併表即貢獻營收 6,700 萬美元，約為併購公告時近 12 個
月平均月營收的一倍。Interplex 為 NVIDIA NVLink Switch 機
殼主要供應商，受惠 AI 機櫃需求強勁，併表貢獻高於原先預估。
考量併購效益優於預期，加上既有業務持續成長，研究部上修 
4Q26 營收至 349.76 億元（+25.5% QoQ、+76.8% YoY，原估 
302.69 億元）。預估毛利率為 32.49%（−0.04ppt QoQ、+0.56ppt 
YoY），大致持平前季；隨營收規模擴大及 Interplex 較低費用
率貢獻，營業費用率降至 10.85%，推升營業利益率至 21.64%
（+0.86ppt QoQ、+3.27ppt YoY，原估 20.0%）。預估 4Q26 稅
後淨利達 55.28 億元（+28.9% QoQ、+101.8% YoY），稀釋後 EPS 
由原估 22.38 元上修至 26.21 元（ +28.9% QoQ、+101.8% YoY）。

3. AI 機櫃供電架構升級，電源單櫃產值與市占率同步提升 
隨 AI 伺服器運算密度持續提高，機櫃功耗由 GB200 世代約 
120kW，朝 Vera Rubin 世代 200kW 以上發展，電力傳輸規格同
步升級。在既有低壓直流架構下，功耗提升帶動電流需求增加，
Power Whip 與 Busbar 須升級至更高載流規格，推升單櫃電源
產值；隨機櫃功率持續攀升，傳統 54VDC 架構面臨銅材用量、配
電損耗及機櫃空間限制，NVIDIA 已推動 800VDC 高壓直流架構，
透過提高配電電壓、減少電力轉換環節，支援未來 MW 級 AI 機
櫃需求。貿聯除受惠電源產品規格升級，於 Vera Rubin 世代的 
Busbar 市占率亦可望明顯提升，推動單櫃電源產值較 GB 世代
倍增。考量新平台相關出貨預計於 1H27 起逐季放量，研究部預
估 2027 年電源營收成長約 114% YoY；中長期隨 800VDC 架構
逐步導入，既有 Power Whip、Busbar 及高電流連接技術可望延
伸至新一代配電系統，持續擴大供應商機。

### Page 3

請參見末頁重要批露及免責聲明  3

4. 高速互連升級至 1.6T，AEC 與高階光纖雙軌成長 
隨 AI 資料中心傳輸頻寬由 800G 朝 1.6T 以上升級，高速互連
對訊號完整性、傳輸距離及功耗的要求持續提高。短距離傳輸仍以
銅纜具成本優勢，較長距離則由光纖滿足頻寬與訊號傳輸需求，預
期 AEC 與光互連將持續並存。AEC 方面，1.6T 新規格因材料成本
及組裝難度提高，產品單價與毛利率可望優於前代，預計於 4Q26 
至 1Q27 逐步導入量產，研究部預估 2027 年 AEC 營收成長約 
41% YoY，主要受惠高階產品占比提升。光纖方面，新富生主要供
應 AI 資料中心機櫃間傳輸所需的 MPO、MT 高密度光纖跳線及連
接器組件，受惠高速光纖互連需求成長，月營收較 2026 年初成長
逾一倍，獲利同步改善。中長期來看，貿聯亦積極布局 Optical 
Shuffle、CPO 交換器方案及 Micro-VCSEL 光互連技術，逐步由傳
統線纜組裝延伸至高階光學組件，為後續成長開拓更多商機。

圖一、貿聯-KY 1Q25～4Q27F HPC 營收結構

資料來源：統一投顧預估

5. 半導體設備需求強勁，非 HPC 業務維持穩健 
貿聯 3Q26 半導體設備營收成長 11% QoQ、70% YoY，為非 HPC 業
務主要成長動能。隨先進製程及半導體設備投資持續推進，客戶需
求由傳統線束延伸至設備模組、精密組裝及次系統整合，帶動單機
供應產值提升。貿聯已深入薄膜沉積與蝕刻設備供應鏈，並逐步拓
展至微影及檢測設備，擴大產品應用範圍。其他業務方面，工廠自
動化、醫療、能源及車用等終端需求維持穩健，提供多元營收來源。

### Page 4

請參見末頁重要批露及免責聲明  4

6. 上修 2026～2028 年獲利預估，營運槓桿與併購效益持續擴
大 
研究部將 Interplex 併表貢獻納入預估，上修貿聯 2026 年營收
至 1,069.96 億元（+50.2% YoY，原估 1,021.18 億元），毛利率
估 31.4%，稀釋後 EPS 73.49 元（+67.4% YoY，原估 68.77 元）。
展望 2027 年，受惠 AI 電源及高速互連業務持續成長，加上 
Interplex 全年併表，預估營收達 1,710.84 億元（+59.9% YoY，
原估 1,517.46 億元）。隨高毛利 HPC 業務占比提升，毛利率可
望升至 34.3%（原估 34.0%）；加上營收規模擴大及 Interplex 
較低費用率貢獻，營業費用率降至 10.2%（原估 13.0%），推升營
業利益率至 24.1%（原估 21.0%），稀釋後 EPS 上修至 145.24 
元（+103.2% YoY，原估 117.82 元）。展望 2028 年，隨 AI 機
櫃供電架構朝 800VDC 演進、高速互連規格持續升級，加上 
Interplex 受惠機櫃需求成長及精密機構件供應範圍擴大，預期將
共同帶動營運成長。研究部預估營收達 2,136.69 億元（+24.9% 
YoY），稀釋後 EPS 196.20 元（+35.1% YoY，原估 171.03 元）。

圖二、貿聯-KY 2025～2028F 各應用別營收占比

資料來源：公司資料及統一投顧預估

7. 中長期成長趨勢明確，投資評等調升至「強力買進」 
研究部持續看好貿聯中長期成長潛力，隨 AI 機櫃電力與高速互
連規格持續升級，帶動單櫃供應產值提升，加上 Interplex 補足
精密機構能力，逐步擴大機櫃次系統供應範圍，有利深化客戶合作
關係並提升市占率。惟考量近期 AI 資料中心建置不確定性升高，
可能使新平台導入及機櫃出貨時程遞延，壓抑市場評價，研究部將
本益比由 25 倍下修至 20 倍。以 2027 年稀釋後 EPS 145.24 
元為評價基礎，目標價 2,950 元，投資評等由「買進」調升至「強
力買進」。

評等 定義 
強力買進    預估未來 6 個月內的絕對報酬超過 25%以上 
買進 預估未來 6 個月內的絕對報酬 10%以上 
中立 預估未來 6 個月內的絕對報酬介於 10～-10% 
降低持股 預估未來 6 個月內的絕對報酬介於(-10%)以下

LG에너지솔루션 
(373220)

ESS 수주 모멘텀 이어진다 Company Brief

3Q26 실적, 원-달러 환율 하락에도 시장 기대치 대폭 상회 
동사의 3Q26 실적은 매출 9.6조원(+59% YoY, +28% QoQ), 영업이익 7,560억원 
(+26% YoY, +568% QoQ)으로 원/달러 환율 하락에도 불구하고 시장기대치(매출 
8.8조원, 영업이익 3,217억원)를 크게 상회했다. 당사는 Stellantis와의 캐나다 
합작관계 종료에 따른 보상금 등을 포함해 약 4,250억원의 일회성 이익이 반영된 
것으로 추정한다. 이를 제외한 영업이익은 약 3,310억원으로, 시장기대치에 대체로 
부합한 것으로 판단된다. AMPC는 미국 ESS 생산량 증가와 GM 얼티엄셀즈 재가동에 
힘입어 전분기 대비 약 73% 증가한 4,169억원을 기록했다. 사업부문별로는 ESS의 
실적 개선이 두드러졌다. 상반기 배터리 패키징 병목으로 이연됐던 물량 출하가 
집중되면서 ESS 출하량은 전분기 대비 60% 가량 증가한 것으로 추정된다. 자동차 
전지도 유럽향 물량 회복과 GM 얼티엄셀즈 재가동으로 매출이 전분기 대비 약 
33%로 증가했으며, 고정비 부담 완화에 따라 적자폭도 뚜렷하게 축소된 것으로 
판단된다. 소형전지는 주요 고객사인 Tesla의 판매 호조에 따른 출하 증가로 매출 
회복과 수익성을 뒷받침한 것으로 보인다. 
4Q26 ESS 중심의 수익성 개선과 수주 모멘텀 지속될 전망 
미국 ESS 시장은 신재생 에너지 확대와 AI 데이터센터의 전력 수요 증가에 힘입어 
빠르게 성장하고 있다. 특히 정책 환경도 비PFE 공급망을 확보한 업체에 유리하게 
작용하고 있다. 동사는 3Q26 실적 컨퍼런스 콜에서 ESS 수주잔고가 약 
160GWh이며, 2028년까지 캐파의 대부분에 대응하는 물량을 확보했다고 밝혔다. 
또한 현재 다수의 고객사들과 2028년 이후 공급 물량을 협의하고 있어 연말까지 
추가적인 수주 모멘텀이 이어질 것으로 예상된다. 이를 바탕으로 4Q26에도 동사의 
본업 실적은 ESS 중심의 개선세를 이어갈 전망이다. 전분기에 반영된 대규모 일회성 
이익의 기저효과로 영업이익은 감소하겠지만, 이를 제외하면 출하량 증가와 가동률 
상승에 따른 점진적인 수익성 개선이 예상된다. 수익성 중심의 사업 효율화를 위해 
소형 IT용 파우치 사업을 축소하고 있다는 점도 중장기 손익 개선에 긍정적이다. 이를 
반영한 4Q26 매출과 영업이익은 각각 약 10.5조원(+63% YoY, +9% QoQ), 
4,150억원(흑자전환 YoY, -45% QoQ)으로 추정된다. 
매수 투자의견과 목표주가 유지 
동사에 대한 매수 투자의견과 목표주가를 유지한다. 북미 ESS 수요 증가와 공급망 
재편에 따른 신규 수주 확대가 중장기 실적 성장을 뒷받침할 전망이다. 특히 ESS용 
생산라인 전환과 가동률 상승이 본격화되면 매출 증가에 더해 고정비 부담 완화와 
AMPC 수혜 확대가 맞물리면서 수익성도 개선될 것으로 예상된다. ESS의 역할이 
신재생 에너지 연계용 저장장치를 넘어 AI 데이터센터의 안정적인 전력 공급을 
지원하는 핵심 인프라로 확대되고 있다는 점에도 주목할 필요가 있다. 이에 따라 
동사의 밸류에이션 역시 기존 EV 중심의 평가에서 벗어나 전력 인프라 산업의 
성장성을 반영하는 방향으로 재평가되어야 한다. 업종 내 실적 개선 가시성과 정책 
수혜가 뚜렷하고, 4Q26 신규 수주에 따른 주가 상승 가능성도 높다는 점에서 동사에 
대한 비중 확대 전략이 유효하다는 판단이다.

Document 277: Anthropic破发一度暴跌14%！创始人套现逾5.6亿美元，黄仁勋回归加剧AI路线之争

Source PDF: all report pdf/Anthropic破发一度暴跌14%！创始人套现逾5.6亿美元，黄仁勋回归加剧AI路线之争.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-11-24
Pages: 4
Status: ok

### Page 1

Anthropic 周⼆盘中⼀度跌⾄ 75.61 美元，市值失守万亿美元，最终收跌 6.55% ⾄ 81.9 美
元，低于 83 美元发⾏价。创始⼈兄妹 Dario 与 Daniela Amodei 在上市次⽇合计套现逾 5.6 亿
美元。⻩仁勋与 Dario ⽴场不同，⻩仁勋⽀持开放模型⽣态，认为不需要为 AI ⾏业额外引⼊
新的监管规定，其重任英伟达 CEO ，让 Anthropic ⾯临的产业竞争格局出现新的变数。
摘要：
Anthropic 上市热潮迅速退去。其在登陆纳斯达克仅三个交易⽇后便跌破 83 美元的 IPO 发⾏价。
创始⼈及⾼管⼤额出售股份、 AI ⾏业估值压⼒，以及⻩仁勋回归后可能加剧的产业路线竞争，共
同成为市场关注焦点。
周⼆美股盘中， Anthropic ⼀度⼤跌 14% ⾄ 75.61 美元，市值跌破万亿美元关⼝，随后有所回
升，最终收跌 6.55% ，报 81.9 美元。
监管⽂件显⽰，CEO Dario Amodei 和总裁 Daniela Amodei 在上市次⽇分别出售超过 337 万股

股票，交易⾦额均超过 2.8 亿美元，两笔交易合计超过 5.6 亿美元。
周⼀⻩仁勋重新担任英伟达 CEO ，也让 Anthropic ⾯临的产业竞争格局出现新的变数。⻩仁勋⻓
期推动开放权重模型⽣态，与 Anthropic 依靠专有模型收费的商业模式存在潜在冲突。双⽅在 AI
监管政策问题上也⽴场不同。
IPO 溢价迅速消退，上市三个交易⽇后跌破发⾏价
Anthropic 于 11 ⽉ 19 ⽇登陆纳斯达克， IPO 发⾏价为 83 美元，⾸个交易⽇开盘价达到 111.50 美
元，收盘时股价仍较发⾏价上涨 17% 。
但上市后的上涨势头未能延续。 Anthropic 股价周⼆下跌 12.6% ⾄ 76.60 美元，跌破 83 美元的发⾏
价。相较⾸⽇开盘价，股价已经明显回落。
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⻅闻历 搜索

### Page 2

此次回撤也发⽣在美国科技股整体承压的背景下。此前⼀周，标普 500 指数和道琼斯⼯业平均指
数均进⼊技术性调整区间，市场对 AI 企业⾼估值以及⼤规模算⼒基础设施投⼊的担忧持续升温。
Anthropic 作为⾸家上市的前沿 AI 企业，其股价表现也被视为观察整个 AI ⾏业投资情绪的重要窗
⼝。
StoneX ⾼级市场分析师 Fiona Cincotta 表⽰，如果数据中⼼建设放缓， Anthropic 开发新模型的
速度可能受到影响，进⽽影响未来收⼊和估值。
Andersen Capital Management 创始⼈ Peter Andersen 则认为，Anthropic 的 IPO 定价可能偏
⾼，⽬前的回落正在推动市场重新发现其合理价值。
创始⼈合计套现逾 5.6 亿美元， IPO 股份供给增加
内部⼈⼠出售股票成为 Anthropic 上市后市场关注的另⼀项⻛险。
据监管⽂件， Dario Amodei 和 Daniela Amodei 在 IPO ⾸个交易⽇之后，分别向此次 IPO 承销商出

售超过 337 万股股票，每笔交易⾦额均超过 2.8 亿美元。
两笔交易合计超过 5.6 亿美元，出售价格均为 83 美元，与 IPO 发⾏价⼀致。
需要注意的是，这些股票并⾮两⼈在⼆级市场直接抛售，⽽是出售给 IPO 承销商。 Anthropic 的
IPO 锁定安排允许部分员⼯和内部⼈⼠从⾸个交易⽇起出售⼀定⽐例的持股，后续股份还将与公
司财报发布等时间节点挂钩，分阶段释放。
此外， IPO 承销商⾏使了额外购买股份的选择权，使 Anthropic 此次 IPO 的募集总额增⾄ 935 亿美
元。
分析指出，这⼀安排虽然进⼀步提⾼了 IPO 募集规模，但也意味着上市后原本可能提供⼀定⽀撑
的额外股份需求已经得到满⾜。
⻩仁勋回归，开放模型路线挑战 Anthropic 商业模式
Anthropic ⾯临的竞争压⼒不仅来⾃资本市场，也来⾃ AI 产业内部不同技术路线之间的分歧。
分析指出，⻩仁勋重新担任英伟达 CEO ，可能给 Anthropic 带来新的竞争压⼒。双⽅的核⼼分歧
在于专有模型与开放权重模型的发展路线。

Anthropic 投⼊⼤量资⾦开发 Claude 系列模型，并对模型保持较严格的控制，客⼾通常通过付费
⽅式使⽤其技术。这⼀模式使模型能⼒、产品服务和商业定价成为公司的核⼼竞争资产。
⻩仁勋则积极推动开放权重模型⽣态。企业可以下载、修改并⾃⾏部署这类模型，⽽不必每次
使⽤ AI 都向模型开发商⽀付费⽤。
英伟达不仅开发⾃⼰的 Nemotron 模型，还⽀持 Reflection AI 、 Thinking Machines 和 Poolside 等
相关初创企业。
对于英伟达⽽⾔，开放模型的普及可能扩⼤ AI 应⽤范围，增加企业⾃⾏部署模型的需求，进⽽带
动 GPU 及相关基础设施销售。但对于 Anthropic ⽽⾔，如果企业越来越多地采⽤开放权重模型，
专有模型的定价能⼒和市场份额可能⾯临压⼒。

### Page 3

⻛险提⽰及免责条款
市场有⻛险，投资需谨慎。本⽂不构成个⼈投资建议，也未考虑到个别⽤⼾特殊的投资⽬标、财务状况或需要。⽤⼾应考虑本⽂
中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。

容为⼀场 “ 冷战 ” 。
Business Insider 指出，随着⻩仁勋重新掌握英伟达的领导权，其开放模型⽣态战略可能得到进
⼀步推进， Anthropic 也将再次⾯对这⼀竞争压⼒。
监管问题上的分歧同样公开化。 Amodei 主张通过加强安全测试、独⽴评估以及领先 AI 企业之间
的协调来控制技术发展节奏。⻩仁勋则认为，现有法律责任机制已经能够发挥作⽤，不需要为 AI
⾏业额外引⼊新的监管规定。
MSCI 纳⼊指数或带来被动资⾦需求

配置需求。
指 数 调 整 研 究 机 构 Intropic 估 计 ， 到 今 年 年 底 ， 被 动 型 基 ⾦ 的 潜 在 买 ⼊ 规 模 可 能 相 当 于
Anthropic 此次发⾏股份的 30% 。
这⼀安排可能为股票带来新增配置需求，但能否抵消市场整体⻛险偏好下降及后续股份供给压
⼒，仍有待观察。
Anthropic 的上市表现显⽰， AI 明星企业进⼊公开市场后，同样需要接受估值、资本开⽀和商业
化能⼒的检验。
⽽随着⻩仁勋重新掌舵英伟达，开放模型与专有模型之间的竞争也可能进⼀步加剧。对于
Anthropic ⽽⾔，如何在保持模型领先的同时证明其商业模式能够持续创造回报，将成为投资者
接下来关注的重点。

Document 303: Cliff Notes_ risks mount (2026-11-25)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Cliff Notes_ risks mount (2026-11-25).pdf
Report source: Westpac IQ
Date: 2026-11-25
Pages: 10
Status: ok

### Page 1

#cliffnotes #rba #australia #inflation #us #fomc #ecb #aud #usd
Cliff Notes: risks mount
By Ryan Wells, Elliot Clarke & Illiana Jain
10:31 November 20 2026 Share
Key insights from the week that was.
Australia’s labour market disappointed again in October, employment falling 43.5k following
September’s 36.4k decline. On a three-month average basis, job gains over the year have now slowed
to 1.1%yr, materially below current growth in the working-age population, 1.7%yr. As a result, the

unemployment rate rose to a new post-pandemic high of 4.9% despite the participation rate falling
another 0.2ppts to 66.6%.

### Page 2

While employment growth’s descent initially reflected softer momentum in hiring, now we are seeing
evidence of a material increase in redundancies. Most affected workers look to have remained in
employment to date, but persistent job shedding raises the risk of a faster and more persistent uptrend
in the unemployment rate, potentially above our baseline peak forecast of 5.2% (at mid-2027) and most
certainly before the late-2028 peak of 5.1% the RBA expects.

As such, the robust 1.1% (3.5%yr) rise in the Q3 Wage Price Index warrants close scrutiny. The Q3
increase was skewed towards those on awards and enterprise arrangements – pay outcomes directly
tied to the Fair Work Commission’s decision and collective bargaining. Individual arrangements
meanwhile, the most responsive to current and expected labour market conditions, accounted for their
smallest share of a Q3 wage increase since 2019. Business survey detail also suggests labour cost

growth will normalise quickly after the award increase.
Deteriorating economic conditions are also increasingly evident in housing. The Westpac Housing
Sentiment Index fell 2.2% to 83.2 in November, among the weakest readings since 2005. Interestingly,
there remains considerable latent demand: almost 13% of consumers intend to purchase a home over
the next year, and relatively few plan to sell. For now, though, weak sentiment, high interest rates and

expectations of further price declines are keeping would-be buyers on the sidelines. These factors also
pose risks to the broader growth story.
In the US, abstracting from the Anthropic IPO, the outlook for inflation remained the critical theme. The
October FOMC minutes highlighted that inflation is currently being supported by tariffs and energy
prices as well as the AI buildout, with "several [members] judg[ing] that price pressures were
increasingly extending beyond industries directly affected by data center construction”. Participants

"assessed that medium- and longer-term inflation expectations continued to be consistent with the
Committee's 2 percent objective", although persistent above-target inflation is a threat to the status
quo, and perceived risks are skewed to the upside. FOMC members who spoke throughout the week
also kept the focus on price risks, and called for the FOMC to act swiftly. Governor Waller again
proposed a 50bp hike in December; others seemingly favour a 25bp hike at the December and January

meetings, as Westpac forecasts, and continuing to tighten further in 2027 if necessary.
Very clearly, the labour market and activity growth are not of concern for US policy makers. Retail sales
gave further justification for this view this week, the control group surprising to the upside with a 0.6%
gain in October after a material upward revision to September (0.5%). That said, housing is under
considerable pressure, pending home sales falling 17.1% over the past year, and housing starts back

at 2019 levels despite a 9.9% rebound in October. Persistent weakness in this sector will, in time, have
implications for both consumption and investment.
China’s October data round was the other key international development of the week. Again, it provided
a call for pro-active stimulus in scale. In the month, annual retail sales growth almost stalled at 0.4%yr
and the decline in fixed asset investment broadened outside property, with capital expenditure now

down 8.1% across the economy year-to-date. Industrial production again highlighted that its output to
satiate global demand which is holding up aggregate growth; though, even here, momentum softened
in October, annual growth in production slowing from 5.5%yr to 4.8%yr. It is not only activity that

### Page 3

Related articles
remains under pressure, but also wealth, with new / existing home prices 0.15% / 0.24% lower in the
month. As we continue to emphasise, to prove effective, stimulus needs to affect change in both activity
and wealth and do so in a sustainable manner.

Document 323: European Economics Analyst_ Euro Area 2027 Outlook_ Resilience Put to the Test

Source PDF: all report pdf/European Economics Analyst_ Euro Area 2027 Outlook_ Resilience Put to the Test.pdf
Report source: Goldman Sachs Publishing
Date: 2026-11-13
Pages: 16
Status: ok

### Page 1

After resilient growth this year, several headwinds will likely weigh on the n
economy in 2027. We expect high energy prices to slow consumption over the 
winter, tighter credit conditions to curb demand and France to stagnate until the 
elections. But global manufacturing (supported by AI capex), German fiscal 
expansion and continued structural expansion in Southern Europe should 
cushion these headwinds. We expect Euro area growth of 0.9% in 2027, below 
the recent run rate, estimated potential and consensus.  
High energy prices will likely keep headline inflation near 4% through the winter, n
while indirect effects push core inflation to a 2.9% peak in Q2. But we expect a 
short-lived hump given limited wage second-round effects, with sequential 
inflation pressures largely normalising in 2027H2. 
Above-target inflation and recent ECB commentary point to one more rate hike n
in December. A March move is possible with higher energy prices and stronger 
data, but the hurdle to faster hikes is high. We expect normalisation cuts from 
2027Q4 as the inflation shock fades, leaving our rate path below market pricing. 
Intensifying French budget tensions are the main risk. Polls suggest that Marine n
Le Pen is most likely to become President. If she is elected and wins a majority in 
Parliament, investors could question her commitment to reduce the deficit. If the 
elections fail to deliver a parliamentary majority, the prospects of continued high 
deficits could further weigh on domestic assets. French fiscal tensions could spill 
over into other high-debt countries, but the risk of area-wide contagion looks 
notably lower than in 2011-15. 
Europe also faces structural headwinds from lost competitiveness, rising n
competition from China and demographic changes. Growth industries (such as 
defence), widespread AI adoption and further internal market integration) offer 
significant opportunities, although the upcoming elections will likely hinder 
major reform progress in the near term.

### Page 2

Euro Area 2027 Outlook: Resilience Put to the Test

Following surprisingly resilient Euro area growth this year, we expect 2027 to be more 
challenging. We forecast area-wide growth of 0.9% next year, slightly below the recent 
run rate, our estimate of potential and consensus (Exhibit 1). But we expect growth to 
improve in H2 as headwinds fade and see significant variation across the Euro area, with 
firm growth in Germany and Spain, subdued momentum in Italy and stagnation in 
France.

Rising Headwinds 
We expect several headwinds to slow growth next year, especially in H1. 
First, high energy prices are likely to weigh on consumption over the winter (Exhibit 2). 
Our commodity strategists expect Brent oil and European TTF gas prices to remain 
elevated given continued disruption of Middle East supplies, with risks towards renewed 
escalation and higher prices. As a result, high headline inflation is likely to weigh 
materially on real disposable income over the winter and slow consumer spending. 
Consistent with this, consumer confidence has dropped since the summer and retail 
sales have weakened, especially in Germany.

Exhibit 1: : We Expect Trend-Like Growth in 2027

Source: Goldman Sachs Global Investment Research, Haver Analytics, Bloomberg
13 November 2026   2
Goldman Sachs European Economics Analyst

### Page 3

Second, tighter credit conditions look set to slow growth (Exhibit 3). Following recent 
easing, our area-wide financial condition index (GSFCI) is now only modestly tighter 
since the summer, as a weaker trade-weighted Euro has mitigated the effects of higher 
long-term yields and wider credit spreads. But survey-based measures of bank lending 
have deteriorated notably, especially in France. As a result, credit conditions are likely to 
act as a moderate headwind to growth next year, particularly on rate-sensitive domestic 
activity (such as housing and business investment).

Third, we expect the French economy to stagnate until the elections (Exhibit 4). In 
addition to tightening financial conditions, heightened political and policy uncertainty 
are likely to lead households and firms to postpone major consumption and investment 
decisions. Fiscal policy is likely to remain a contractionary force, because a large 
increase in interest payments will likely mask a modest consolidation in the primary 
balance. We forecast annual average growth of 0.1% in 2027, well below other

Exhibit 2: Higher Energy Prices are Likely to Weigh on Consumption

Source: Goldman Sachs Global Investment Research, Haver Analytics, Bloomberg

Exhibit 3: Tighter Credit Conditions Will Weigh on Growth

Source: Goldman Sachs Global Investment Research, Haver Analytics
13 November 2026   3
Goldman Sachs European Economics Analyst

### Page 4

forecasters and the weakest forecast among the developed economies we cover.

Sources of Resilience 
That said, we see several sources of resilience, which should buffer these headwinds and 
help growth recover in H2. 
First, we expect a continued boost from the global manufacturing cycle (Exhibit 5). The 
international industrial upswing continues, boosting factory orders and exports, 
especially in Germany. AI capex is an important driver behind this pickup, likely lifting 
Euro area growth by 0.1pp this year and next. Given spare capacity in European 
manufacturing—particularly in Germany—we see a continued cyclical export tailwind 
ahead.

Second, fiscal policy will continue to support growth in Germany (Exhibit 6). We remain 
constructive on the German fiscal rollout driven by higher defence spending and

Exhibit 4: We Expect Stagnation in France as Fiscal Uncertainty Builds

Source: Goldman Sachs Global Investment Research, Haver Analytics

Exhibit 5: A Continued Boost from Global Manufacturing, Including AI Capex

Source: Goldman Sachs Global Investment Research, Haver Analytics
13 November 2026   4
Goldman Sachs European Economics Analyst

### Page 5

improved execution of infrastructure investment. We expect the German deficit to rise 
to 4.6% next year (from 4% in 2026) and boost German growth by 0.6pp (vs 0.7pp this 
year). That said, fiscal policy looks set to turn contractionary in France and Spain as 
national consolidation pressures build and the Recovery Fund expires. As a result, we see 
a neutral area-wide fiscal stance in 2027 after a small net boost this year, before turning 
contractionary in 2028.

Third, we see continued structural expansion in Southern Europe (Exhibit 7). Spain, in 
particular, has continued to outgrow the Euro area materially, driven by strong gains in 
employment (underpinned by immigration), higher-value-added services (including 
finance and ICT) and investment. While momentum is likely to moderate next year, we 
see continued outperformance in the South.

### Page 6

Modest Underlying Inflation Pressures 
High energy prices (including product spreads) are likely to keep headline inflation close 
to 4% through the winter (Exhibit 8). Headline inflation could climb even higher if upside 
risks to our commodity price forecasts materialise, with headline inflation peaking close 
to 6% in an adverse scenario for oil and gas. We also see building pressures on food 
inflation next year, reflecting passthrough effects from higher energy prices, the ongoing 
Russia/Ukraine war and global weather patterns.

We do not, however, look for material passthrough effects from headline to underlying 
inflation (Exhibit 9). So far, we see little evidence of indirect effects of higher energy 
prices into core inflation, with muted movement in energy-intensive core goods prices. 
Instead, we estimate that most of the recent pickup in core goods inflation reflects 
AI-related equipment. Given pipeline pressures in import and producer prices as well as 
airfares, we expect more notable indirect effects to build in coming months, pushing 
core inflation up.

Exhibit 8: A Sharp Pickup in Headline Inflation

Source: Goldman Sachs Global Investment Research, Haver Analytics
13 November 2026   6
Goldman Sachs European Economics Analyst

### Page 7

But we expect the core inflation hump to be short-lived given signs of limited 
second-round effects (Exhibit 10). Measures of wage growth have remained contained 
despite the surge in energy prices, including forward-looking surveys (such as the Indeed 
and ECB wage trackers) and recent wage agreements. While we have slightly raised our 
forecast for nominal wage growth next year, we expect pay gains to return to their 
normal range in 2028, driven by moderating labour demand.

Taken together, we expect core inflation to peak at 2.9% in Q2, significantly above the 
ECB’s target and somewhat above the recent staff projections (Exhibit 11), partly 
reflecting firmer energy price assumptions. But we expect the overshoot to be notably 
more limited and temporary than in 2022-23, with sequential inflation pressures largely 
normalising in 2027H2.

Exhibit 9: Modest Passthrough Effects Ahead

Source: Bloomberg, Goldman Sachs Global Investment Research

Exhibit 10: Little Risk of Second-Round Effects

Source: Goldman Sachs Global Investment Research, Haver Analytics, ECB

Goldman Sachs European Economics Analyst

Limited ECB Tightening 
Above-target inflation, ongoing growth and signs that the neutral rate might have edged 
up point to additional ECB tightening, consistent with recent ECB communication and 
our macro model simulations (Exhibit 12). We therefore view one more hike at the 
December meeting as likely. Acute sovereign stress could still lead the Council to hold 
rates in December, but the required tightening in financial conditions would likely need 
to be large given the ongoing energy pressures.

An additional hike in March is possible if energy prices continue to rise, core inflation 
firms more than expected, or clearer signs of second-round effects emerge. But we see a 
significant hurdle to speeding up the pace of hikes and raising the deposit rate 
meaningfully above 3%. We expect the Governing Council to start normalising the policy 
rate as the inflation shock fades and sequential core inflation normalises, with cuts in

Exhibit 11: A Limited Inflation Overshoot

Source: Goldman Sachs Global Investment Research, Haver Analytics

Exhibit 12: A December Hike is Very Likely

Source: Goldman Sachs Global Investment Research, Haver Analytics
13 November 2026   8
Goldman Sachs European Economics Analyst

### Page 9

December 2027 and March 2028. As a result, our policy rate path remains below market 
pricing, including on a probability-weighted basis.

Risks from a French Budget Crisis 
An intensification of French budget tensions is the main downside risk to our outlook. 
French bonds could already come under stress before the elections if presidential 
candidates do not express sufficient concern regarding the fiscal outlook. Markets would 
probably react negatively to far-left leader Mélenchon progressing to the second round 
of the presidential elections, even though polls suggest he would then be very likely to 
lose against far-right leader Marine Le Pen. Investors could also question Le Pen’s 
commitment to reducing the deficit if she becomes President—as polls currently suggest 
she will—and wins a parliamentary majority. In that respect, we noted that her recent 
economic proposals would likely face significant implementation challenges, as they are 
based on optimistic growth assumptions and include costly measures. Finally, if no 
parliamentary majority emerges after the next elections—to which our model attributes 
a 20% probability— bond markets would likely react negatively to the prospect of 
continued high deficits, political uncertainty and low growth. As a result, we expect 
French government bonds to remain under pressure.

Exhibit 13: Our ECB Views Remain Dovish Compared with Market Pricing

Source: Goldman Sachs Global Investment Research, ECB
13 November 2026   9
Goldman Sachs European Economics Analyst

### Page 10

An escalation of French budget tensions could spill over into other high-debt bond 
markets (Exhibit 15). Fiscal imbalances are elevated, interest rates are high, and debt 
dynamics look concerning in several countries (including Italy, Belgium, Austria and 
Finland). Financial-market vulnerabilities also persist through sovereign exposures and 
non-bank leverage. Upcoming elections in Italy may complicate the fiscal adjustment 
required, while Spain’s fiscal outlook remains encouraging (despite the November 29 
election). Also, failure to agree on the 2028-35 EU Budget could bring into question 
fiscal cohesion.

That said, the Euro area today is stronger in several important respects than during the 
European sovereign debt crisis (Exhibit 16). Nominal growth is materially higher, 
household finances are more robust, external imbalances are smaller, and banks—
especially in Southern Europe—are much healthier. The Euro area has also strengthened 
its institutional architecture through the ESM and greater common EU issuance.

Moreover, the ECB could activate its anti-fragmentation tools in the event of extreme 
bond market pressures (Exhibit 17), including the Transmission Protection Instrument 
(TPI). But our analysis suggests that ECB intervention would likely require significantly 
more intense and broad-based sovereign stress, especially with inflation well above 
target. In particular, our new index—constructed to predict ECB bond market 
interventions—suggests that fragmentation risk remains limited despite the recent 
widening in spreads.

Structural Challenges and Opportunities  
Europe continues to struggle with several structural headwinds. The Euro area has lost 
international competitiveness and faces rising competition with China, which is likely to 
act as a steady headwind and push the EU towards a more assertive trade stance. Also, 
Europe has underinvested in growth sectors, offers a less attractive business

That said, we also see significant growth opportunities through reforms, as outlined in

through well-targeted public investment (including defence), leadership in growth

improvement in Europe’s economic performance. We will, in particular, monitor efforts

Document 331: Following the oil shock, El Niño threatens to hurt macroeconomic resilience in Sub-Saharan Africa further (2026-10-21)

for a changing

FOLLOWING THE OIL SHOCK, EL NIÑO THREATENS TO HURT MACROECONOMIC RESILIENCE IN SUB-SAHARAN
AFRICA FURTHER
Lucas Plé
Seven months after the start of the war in the Middle East, which has led to a sharp rise in oil prices, sub-Saharan Africa is holding up well
overall. The deterioration in external accounts and the rise in inflation are more contained than in 2022, during the last oil shock. However,
this resilience is fragile and is eroding as the conflict drags on. Furthermore, an exceptional El Niño event could significantly worsen the

macroeconomic environment in 2027 1.
Economic growth remains robust
For countries with available data, GDP growth held up well in the first half of 2026 2. In mid-October, the IMF revised its regional forecast for
the full year upwards to 4.5% (+0.2 percentage points compared with April), in line with the performance for 2025.
1 El Niño 2026–2027: multiple pressures on prices and supplies
2 1.4% year-on-year (y/y) in South Africa, 4.2% in Nigeria, 5.1% in Kenya, 7% in Angola, 7.3% in Ghana.

Sub-Saharan Africa is highly vulnerable to climate shocks via the agricultural commodities channel

### Page 2

2
The bank for
a changing
world
Chart of the Week / 21 October 2026 economic-research.bnpparibas.com
A mitigated shock on terms of trade
High prices for export commodities (gold, copper, platinum) have helped to cushion the impact on terms of trade and the external
accounts of hydrocarbon-importing countries. For the 10 countries in our sample (see heatmap), the depreciation of their currencies
against the US dollar since last February has been moderate overall (2.3% on average).

Inflation has started to rise again, but it is still being contained so far
In the ten countries in our sample, although inflation is rising again, it is lower year-on-year for the first eight months of 2026 than it was
for the same period in 2022. This is due to:
1. Monetary policies that were still restrictive before the conflict started;
2. Fuel subsidies;
3.   Macroeconomic reforms implemented in recent years (a return to orthodox monetary policy in Nigeria, and efforts towards fiscal

consolidation in many countries);
4. Moderate currency depreciation since February.
As a result, few central banks have tightened their monetary policy since February (South Africa, Uganda and Tanzania).
However, this resilience is still fragile…
On the one hand, governments that have chosen to cap fuel price rises are seeing their public finances deteriorate. In Senegal, energy
subsidies will go above 3% of GDP in 2026, which is unsustainable. On the other hand, the region’s external accounts are exposed to

volatile global commodity prices and tighter international financing conditions.
… and the macroeconomic environment could quickly worsen in 2027, due to an exceptional El Niño event
The region is particularly vulnerable to climate shocks via the agricultural commodities channel. Given agriculture’s high share in its GDP,
sub-Saharan Africa is more exposed to downside risks to growth than Latin America, Asia or Central Europe (1 st column of the heatmap).

It is also more exposed than other regions of the world to the inflationary effect of rising food prices, given their higher share in the
consumer basket (2 nd column). Finally, even though its dependence on food imports is lower than in Asia and Europe (3 rd column), the
region is highly vulnerable to rising international food commodity prices due to its fragile external accounts. For several countries in our
sample, foreign exchange reserves cover less than four months’ worth of imports of goods and services (Uganda, Tanzania, Ghana and

Zambia), leaving little room for manoeuvre for absorbing a further external shock.
In addition to shocks via the agricultural commodities channel, other localised risks must be considered. In Southern Africa, El Niño is
expected to intensify droughts and threaten electricity generation as a result, particularly in Zambia (where hydropower accounts
for nearly 80% of domestic electricity generation). In East Africa, the increased flooding that El Niño is expected to cause will threaten

infrastructure – particularly logistics infrastructure – which is already less efficient than the global average 3. These two sources of
vulnerability could also increase pressure on growth, prices and the external accounts.
In conclusion, if oil prices remain high and El Niño is exceptionally severe, the region will struggle to absorb this double shock in 2027.
Despite their status as net exporters of hydrocarbons, Nigeria and Angola are also exposed to a deterioration in the macroeconomic

environment in 2027. Only South Africa appears to be better positioned to absorb the shock.
lucas.ple@bnpparibas.com
3 See the World Bank’s Logistics Performance Index.

Document 332: France’s golden rule won’t fix its budget problem

Source PDF: all report pdf/France’s golden rule won’t fix its budget problem.pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 5
Status: ok

### Page 1

French President Emmanuel Macron’s proposal to revive a
constitutional ﬁscal rule has helped ease pressure on French
government bonds. But the reform would only create a
framework whose strength would depend on legislation
adopted later. The immediate test of France’s ﬁscal credibility
remains the 2027 budget
Charlotte de Montpellier
Macron’s golden rule may reassure markets, but it cannot replace a credible
ﬁscal plan. The immediate test will be France’s 2027 budget

What exactly would change?
Macron’s surprise proposal came at the start of another high-risk
week for France, after the European Commission forecast last week
that the deﬁcit would reach 5.4% of GDP in 2027 and 5.7% in 2028,
and ahead of S&P’s rating assessment on Friday. It would revive a
constitutional amendment approved by the National Assembly
and the Senate in 2011 – during the eurozone sovereign debt crisis
– but never submitted to Congress for ﬁnal approval.

Your privacy is important
At ING we respect your right to choose how your data is used.
You can review and change your preferences at any time in
our Cookie policy.
Essential
Performance Cookies
Targeting Cookies
Save preferences Accept all

### Page 2

The reform would create a new category of legislation: multi-year
ﬁscal framework laws. Parliament would adopt a framework
covering at least three years and setting a path towards ﬁscal
balance, including annual spending ceilings and minimum revenue
measures. Annual state and social security budgets would then
have to comply with it. This would be an important change from
France’s existing public ﬁnance programming laws. These have set
multi-year ﬁscal trajectories since 2009 but do not legally constrain

annual budgets. Under the proposed system, the Constitutional
Council could reject a budget that was inconsistent with the
applicable framework law.
However, the constitutional amendment would not determine how
quickly the deﬁcit must fall, set a debt target or a numerical ceiling
comparable with Germany’s debt brake. It would create the legal
architecture for a ﬁscal rule rather than deﬁne the rule itself.
Its actual strength would be determined by a subsequent organic

law specifying how compliance was assessed, how deviations were
treated, what corrective action was required and which exceptional
circumstances could justify a departure from the trajectory. Weak
provisions would produce a weak rule, regardless of its
constitutional status.
There is another important source of ﬂexibility. Parliament could
modify the trajectory at any point by adopting a new framework
law, for instance after an economic crisis or a change of

government. Of course, some ﬂexibility is important, but it also
creates an obvious risk: the framework law could simply be
rewritten whenever the annual budget no longer complies with it. A
rule revised every year would not be signiﬁcantly more credible
than the programming laws France already has (and ignores).
A long and uncertain political process
Approving this “golden rule” reform would require the support of
three-ﬁfths of the votes cast at a joint sitting of the National

Assembly and Senate. The centre, the right and the far right
broadly support the principle of a golden rule. On current
parliamentary arithmetic, however, they would still fall around 30
Your privacy is important
At ING we respect your right to choose how your data is used.
You can review and change your preferences at any time in
our Cookie policy.
Essential
Performance Cookies
Targeting Cookies

### Page 3

votes short, which would require additional support from
independent lawmakers.
Even if Macron secured the necessary majority, constitutional
approval would only be the ﬁrst stage. Parliament would still have
to adopt the organic law deﬁning the mechanism, and then a
framework law establishing the actual ﬁscal trajectory. The system
could not apply to the 2027 budget and is very unlikely to be
operational for the 2028 budget.
France’s problem is not a shortage of rules

France is already subject to several layers of ﬁscal rules. National
programming laws set deﬁcit and debt trajectories, while the
European framework includes the excessive deﬁcit procedure
opened against France in 2024. Yet these rules have repeatedly
failed to constrain annual budgets. A stronger ﬁscal framework
could improve predictability and make repeated deviations more
diﬃcult. The experience of Germany, Switzerland and Sweden
shows that well-designed rules can support ﬁscal discipline when

they are backed by a suﬃciently broad and lasting political
consensus.
France’s central problem, however, is less the absence of rules than
the absence of political willingness to implement them. Successive
governments have become adept at presenting medium-term
consolidation plans and subsequently postponing the adjustment.
Sure, a constitutional rule could make such promises more diﬃcult
to disregard. But it could not ensure that the initial trajectory was

realistic, prevent Parliament from rewriting it or create the political
agreement required to adopt the underlying spending and tax
measures.
The budget comes ﬁrst
The immediate signal expected is therefore not a constitutional
reform that might become operational several years from now, but
the adoption of a credible budget. The 2027 budget is now being
examined by the Senate, where the centre-right majority wants to
add around €6bn in spending cuts to the government’s initial

Your privacy is important
At ING we respect your right to choose how your data is used.
You can review and change your preferences at any time in
our Cookie policy.
Essential
Performance Cookies
Targeting Cookies

### Page 4

proposal. Further consolidation is needed to bring the deﬁcit down
to 5% of GDP next year, as the macroeconomic outlook has
deteriorated and more than €1.5bn in additional spending
measures have been announced since the draft budget was
presented. The government has indicated that the ﬁnal adjustment
could amount to €6-8bn, on top of the €43bn already announced.
The text will then return to the National Assembly, where the
government lacks a majority and cannot rely on an ordinary vote. It

will therefore have to choose between Article 49.3 and ordinances.
Under Article 49.3, the amended budget would be adopted unless
an absolute majority of MPs backed a motion of no conﬁdence. If
such a motion passed, the government would fall and the budget
would be rejected. The government could seek a non-censure
agreement with the Socialist Party or the National Rally, but no
negotiations have started so far. Alternatively, it could proceed
without an agreement and bet that the opposition parties would

fail to unite behind the same motion, a particularly risky strategy.
If Parliament has not completed its examination by midnight on 17
December, the government could instead enact the budget by
ordinance from 18 December. A subsequent motion of no
conﬁdence could still bring down the government, but the budget
would remain in force. This would remove the risk of France entering
2027 without a budget, but it would reinstate the initial draft, which

does not contain the additional measures needed to reach the 5%
deﬁcit target. Article 49.3 would therefore oﬀer greater ﬁscal
credibility if successful, but with a signiﬁcant risk that France ends
up without a budget. Ordinances would ensure that a budget
enters into force, but at the cost of a weaker ﬁscal plan. The
government increasingly appears to favour Article 49.3, although
without a negotiated non-censure agreement, this remains a high-
risk option.

All in all, a golden rule may eventually help France remain on a
credible ﬁscal path. But it cannot replace the decisions needed to
establish that path. The most urgent question is whether the
government can put a credible budget in place without triggering
another political crisis.

We have published our 2027 Macro Outlook: Steady Baseline, Downside Risks. We flesh 
out the Global Market implications and key asset views here, highlighting 10 
investment themes that are likely to dominate price action in 2027. More detailed 
outlooks for the individual asset classes are forthcoming.  
A maturing market cycle: Stable global growth and declining inflation make for 1.
a friendly macro baseline, but risks are to the downside as the market cycle ages. 
The AI investment impulse peaks: AI has contributed more to market gains 2.

or markets look further ahead.

near-term risks to markets.

Goldman Sachs Global Markets Outlook 2027

### Page 3

Markets Outlook 2027: Growing Pains

1. A maturing market cycle 
Stable growth, declining inflation make for a friendly macro backdrop. n
But risks are to the downside, and an ageing market cycle is facing more headwinds. n
Higher rates, wider spreads, and a peaking AI impulse mean the broad asset picture n
is more two-sided ... 
... and argues for more diversified allocations, including bonds and hedges. n
Our headline macro forecasts for 2027 are quite friendly: stable growth, declining 
inflation and peaking policy rates. But as the economic cycle extends, the market cycle is 
increasingly showing its age. For 2026, our view was that even though asset market 
valuations were running ahead of the macro, a positive cyclical backdrop including 
strong investment growth should dominate. While the Iran war has upended many 
forecasts, even without the energy shock the market is running into more conventional 
late-cycle headwinds. Real rates are higher, credit spreads are wider and volatility is 
picking up, especially under the surface. On top of this, the AI impulse, which has been a 
key support to markets through the shocks this year, looks poised to peak in the coming 
year. 
That is likely to prove a bigger headwind to the profit and asset market picture than 
growth, given its larger role in supporting equity markets. Markets have already moved 
some way towards reflecting that the extraordinary surge in profitability in the AI 
infrastructure complex is unlikely to be extrapolated. So a flattening in the gains there 
may be offset by gains further down the waterfall among AI users or other cyclical parts 
of the market, especially if energy prices and rates fall more. But that is a tricky 
balancing act, and further boosts to equity prices are likely to come alongside a higher 
base level of volatility. There are also risks that AI-related drawdowns tighten financial 
conditions more meaningfully, or that energy and policy relief do not arrive on time. 
That leaves the broader risk-asset picture looking more two-sided than it has for several 
years, arguing for more diversified and better-hedged portfolios. 
12 November 2026   3
Goldman Sachs Global Markets Outlook 2027

### Page 4

2. The AI investment impulse peaks 
The AI investment impulse peak is a bigger test for markets than the economy. n
That impulse has been a larger contributor to growth in market value and earnings n
than in GDP. 
Markets have already de-rated AI infrastructure beneficiaries. n
Valuation excesses are less visible, so the focus is on sustainability of record profits. n
Coming into 2026, we expected continued acceleration in AI investment to dominate 
valuation concerns in equities, but likely amid higher volatility and wider credit spreads.  
Although we expect solid AI investment growth to continue, our forecasts imply that the 
investment growth impulse—additional AI investment as a share of GDP—will peak in 
early 2027. From a macro perspective, this should be a manageable event. But it may be 
a bigger test for markets for three reasons. First, the AI impulse has been a much larger 
contributor to growth in earnings and market value than to growth in GDP. Second, 
AI-related equities have taken credit for significant macro productivity gains, especially 
companies benefiting directly from the infrastructure boom, that have made them more 
vulnerable to any signs that we are on a less optimistic path. Third, the point at which 
growth rates peak (a “second derivative” turning point) often matters, even if underlying 
growth rates (the “first derivative”) remain healthy. Moving through that peak allows 
markets to place limits on the upside of an uncertain distribution and, as growth 
decelerates, the period of positive surprises to expectations often ends too, as we may 
be starting to see for the AI capex cycle. 
Markets have already been reflecting these concerns. Since June, we have seen a 
significant de-rating of companies levered to the AI infrastructure theme, which are 
most sensitive to shifts in the investment outlook and had been the biggest market 
beneficiaries in the first half of 2026. Moreover, robust profits, and a rising share of

income going to listed companies, have helped keep both the imbalances and the deep 
valuation excesses that we saw in the late 1990s at bay. The key question in this cycle is 
whether the market is correctly assessing how much of the recent acceleration in profits, 
which has taken S&P 500 earnings to a record high relative to GDP, will prove persistent, 
especially as the investment cycle itself fades. Without the capital inflows from the EM 
crises in 1997 and 1998—or the rate cuts that goods price deflation allowed during that 
tech cycle—increased financing needs have also been putting clearer upward pressure 
on the cost of capital.

3. Passing—or dropping—the baton 
Strong levels of AI investment growth and profitability should limit the pressure. n
And the baton may be passed from AI infrastructure to other market areas. n
But we see risks of a larger aggregate shock if the market looks forward, or other n
areas cannot take up the slack. 
Tighter financial conditions or credit conditions could create a feedback loop. n
Two things give us comfort in terms of how bumpy this transition through the peak of 
the investment cycle will prove to be. The first is that although the investment impulse 
from AI looks poised to peak, growth in both AI investment and earnings should remain 
firmly positive in 2027, especially earlier in the year. We expect S&P 500 earnings growth 
to decelerate from 36% in 2026 to a still-robust 11% in 2027. This may ultimately help to 
limit the de-rating of even the most directly affected areas and should reduce the 
impact on broader growth-sensitive assets, though it should also imply less relief in core 
rates pricing. The second is that the baton may be passed, at least to a degree, from the 
direct beneficiaries of the AI investment boom to other areas of the market. This 
includes other areas of the AI complex, such as the hyperscalers, if monetization paths 
become more visible; companies that may begin to show benefits from AI productivity 
enhancements; or non-AI companies that see some relief as the crowding out of capital

available to them eases. 
The risk is that what could be more of a rotation—and a flattening in the upward trend in 
asset markets—becomes a bigger aggregate shock. This could happen because the 
market begins to look further forward to the peak in the investment share itself (in 2028 
on our forecasts); because the passing of the baton to other sectors does not take place 
as quickly or as smoothly as needed; or because feedback loops from financial and 
credit conditions accelerate more quickly than in our baseline. We think the risk to 
markets is higher than the risk to the economic picture on each front, because the boom 
itself has had its largest impact there. For that reason, the most likely channel through 
which AI worries could lead to broader economic downside is through financial 
conditions themselves.

4. Energy—balancing risk and risk premium 
An escalation-driven oil spike remains one of the largest near-term risks to markets. n
Our baseline is for a gradual decline in energy prices, but the hedge value of energy n
is still high. 
Downside tail to energy prices with clearer/faster resolution. n
Risk premium has fluctuated, so hedge value also varies over time. n
A potential oil price spike driven by another escalation in the war with Iran remains one 
of the biggest near-term risks to the markets and growth picture. Particularly after the 
fall in oil prices so far in November, pricing is vulnerable to any broadening in the conflict 
and new supply disruptions. We would expect that to hurt equities, support the Dollar 
against European currencies and put upward pressure on rates, all else equal. Europe, 
too, faces a precarious winter, given low storage levels in natural gas which leave the 
region vulnerable to risks from both supply and weather. If those risks are avoided, our 
baseline is for a gradual decline in energy prices over the coming year even without full 
resolution of the conflict. Despite the ongoing war with Iran, oil market supply has 
largely been restored (even if refining capacity is limited), but risks of escalation and 
further disruptions have generally led the market to demand a fluctuating but 
meaningful risk premium above where we think the fundamental supply-demand picture 
would leave them.

### Page 7

Our baseline is a gradual recovery in supply and reduction in energy prices, leaving 
energy and core commodity returns more muted. But the risk of more serious 
disruptions is real, and energy prices could move significantly higher in those scenarios 
at a time when equities and, potentially, bonds could both be under pressure. The 
hedging value of the asset class remains high for that reason. The wrinkle is that the risk 
premium in oil has varied substantially and conditions the value of the hedge. When the 
risk premium falls, as it has done more recently, that hedging value is likely to be higher. 
When that risk premium is very high, as it was at the end of October, oil markets are 
more vulnerable to downside tails if news flow is better than expected, and outright 
longs (rather than options) have generally been more risky. The downside tail from faster 
progress towards resolution than we expect (not just in Iran, but in Ukraine too) is one of 
the clearest sources of upside risks for broader markets. In that scenario, energy prices 
could come down more quickly, supporting both bonds and equities.

5. Bonds reclaim macro and hedging value 
Rates moved up in 2026, on oil, hikes, AI spending and fiscal deficits. n
Central bank pricing now too hawkish relative to our baseline. n
Bonds should be a better hedge as supply shocks give way to demand stresses. n
Higher running yield, more paths to relief, and cheap valuations argue for higher n
bond allocations. 
Interest rates moved higher in 2026 after a low point on the eve of the Iran war. The 
resultant surge in energy prices and central bank pricing has been the primary catalyst 
across most DM and EM rates markets, but real rates, especially in the US, have 
continued to move higher as investment demand from the AI build-out interacts with 
sustained fiscal deficits. Despite that backdrop, we think that central bank pricing in 
many economies, including the US and the UK, is too hawkish relative to what is likely to 
be delivered. Even with the energy shock, the underlying inflation story is still fairly 
benign, which means that policy rates are likely to peak at lower levels relative to 
2023-2024 and reverse earlier. The risk to this view is that the energy shock extends 
further or inflation remains stickier and the labour market proves more resilient, as it did 
this year. 
Bonds have been a poor hedge in this environment of energy supply shocks, rate hikes

### Page 8

and real rate pressures from private and public sector spending. But we think the case 
for duration has shifted in a more constructive direction for several reasons. First, on a 
static basis, bond yields are now providing a good running yield and a decent spread to 
cash. Second, valuations are cheap (we see UST 10y yields at 4.6% by end-2027), 
positioning is underweight, and there are paths to some shallow rate relief from current 
levels under our benign baseline scenario, and scope for larger moves if AI spending 
decelerates more quickly. Third, the increase in yields has started to create stresses that 
indicate that a higher level of real rates may be difficult to sustain. Bonds are already 
starting to illustrate their value as a hedge against shocks of these types—with Bunds 
rallying as French sovereign spreads widened and USTs rallying as tech stocks came 
under pressure. Although energy spikes remain a near-term risk, we are more positive 
about bond allocations even after the recent rally, in part because of that hedging value.

6. A more cyclical Dollar, Asia’s revaluing currencies 
After tight ranges in 2026, lower inflation, energy prices make for a softer Dollar. n
But a bigger USD swing could come if US tech exceptionalism erodes. n
JPY is a more attractive hedge with ‘team Japan’ focused on limiting Yen weakness. n
CNY appreciation has further to run given undervaluation and a surging trade n
surplus.  
The broad Dollar is finishing 2026 on the strong side of tight ranges, buoyed by a sturdy 
US nominal growth backdrop, higher energy prices, and a shift to more hawkish Fed 
pricing. Under our baseline forecast, all three impulses should fade: alongside trend 
growth we expect inflation in the US to converge towards target, energy prices should 
decline as the Middle East disruptions gradually normalize and we expect the Fed to 
move from hikes to cuts as we head into late 2027 and early 2028. That should make for
